Text drafts and Quick capture's text are localStorage items mirrored to IndexedDB (`mirroredItem`: both copies carry a revision, the newer wins at start; deletions are recorded) because Chromium (WebView2, Android WebView) commits localStorage 1–5 s or, rate-limited, minutes after a write and drops what is pending when killed, and WebKitGTK commits after 500 ms; a completed IndexedDB transaction survives a kill. Only the main page writes `tf.drafts` (`startDrafts`). Images, large pastes and uploaded videos' references are IndexedDB slots; a video cut off mid-upload comes back failed ("attach it again"). A page with an upload in flight reports it (`drafts_busy`): an automatic update waits as if the user were in front.
- **Drag and drop.** The main window has `dragDropEnabled: false` (base, Linux and macOS configs; the platform files replace the `windows` array): Tauri's native drag-drop handler would block every HTML5 file drop, so the session composer, Dispatch and the terminal (paste-by-path uploads) get dropped files from the webview. A file dropped outside a drop zone is refused app-wide (`src/features/shell/fileDrops.ts`, installed in `main.tsx`: `dropEffect` "none") so the webview never opens it in place of the app. Nothing uses `tauri://drag-drop`.
- **Windows:**
  - Custom chrome via `TitleBar`; Snap Layouts via `tauri-plugin-snap-layout`.
  - Tray icon with the needs-you count, taskbar overlay badge and progress bar (only Lively animates the tray comet and the progress marquee, §6.3), single instance, deep link `teleflock://`.
  - Toast notifications with actions; retracted from Action Center (tag + group) when handled elsewhere, user dismissal reported to the engine.
- **Linux.**
  - Notifications go over D-Bus to `org.freedesktop.Notifications` (zbus, already in the build; no libdbus, no new package dependency): buttons, KDE inline reply and the body click (`default`) per the server's capabilities, replaced per agent via `replaces_id`, closed on `retract` while the tag still shows them, user dismissal (`NotificationClosed` reason 2) reported to the engine.
  - `supports_notifications()` is true once a server answered the startup probe (on a thread of its own).
  - Editing keys a virtual keyboard sends (`edit_keys.rs`, main and Quick capture webviews). WebKitGTK turns Ctrl+V, Shift+Insert, Backspace … into editing commands through GTK key bindings, which look a key up by keycode; GTK 3's Wayland keymap never lists the keymap's highest keycode (`gdk_wayland_keymap_get_entries_for_keyval` stops one short), and wtype-style virtual keyboards (the virtual-keyboard protocol dictation tools use on wlroots desktops) put the last key they send there. A lone paste chord or Backspace from such a tool therefore bound to nothing: the paste after a dictation tool copied its text, or its corrections, never happened (typed text was unaffected). WebKit hands a key event nothing handled back to GTK; a key-press after-handler on the webview then runs the command itself: Ctrl+V, Ctrl+Shift+V, Shift+Insert → `Paste`; Backspace → `DeleteBackward`; Delete → `DeleteForward`. Keys the page prevented or whose binding ran never come back, so nothing runs twice.
- **macOS.**
  - Notifications use `UNUserNotificationCenter` (`objc2-user-notifications`) in bundled builds only (an unbundled binary has no bundle id and the API would raise). They ask for the permission once at startup, replace in place per agent (request id = tag), carry their buttons in per-action-set categories with a custom dismiss action, and are cleared on quit.
  - `supports_notifications()` is true when bundled and not denied.
  - Microphone: `Info.plist` (merged into the bundle's by the bundler) carries `NSMicrophoneUsageDescription` (dictation and browser recordings; macOS refuses the microphone to an app without it) and `NSSpeechRecognitionUsageDescription` (the webview's speech recognition); `Entitlements.plist` (`bundle.macOS.entitlements`) grants `com.apple.security.device.audio-input`, without which the hardened runtime withholds the microphone without asking.
- **System presence (desktop).** `Platform::system_presence()` supplies the OS-wide idle time and screen lock the engine merges with `app.presence` (`idle.rs`): `GetLastInputInfo` + desktop-switch probe on Windows, `CGEventSourceSecondsSinceLastEventType` on macOS, GNOME Mutter's `IdleMonitor` / KDE's `ScreenSaver` (+ `GetActive` for the lock) over D-Bus on Linux; unknown where a desktop offers none.
- **Android.** A Kotlin plugin provides:
  - a foreground service (`remoteMessaging`) holding the core alive while `keepAlive`, also after the activity is gone (closed from recents). `MainActivity` reports its lifecycle over JNI: without an activity, Kotlin commands (notifications included) are skipped, and the next activity gets the main window rebuilt, since Tauri creates windows once per process (`src-tauri/README.md`, "App closed from recents");
  - notifications with action buttons (≤ 3) and inline reply, routed back to `agent.answer` / `agent.prompt`; cancelled by tag + id when handled elsewhere (group summary follows), swipe-away reported over JNI;
  - `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` permission flow and haptics;
  - dictation for the composer's mic: the `RECORD_AUDIO` permission flow (asked on the first tap, explained after a denial, **Open settings** once Android blocks the prompt) and `android.speech.SpeechRecognizer`, its partial and final phrases streamed to the webview over a channel, since Android's WebView has no working Web Speech API (`src-tauri/README.md`). The Android app has no on-device engine (tf-dictation is not in its build); the desktop apps dictate on the device once a speech model is chosen (§6.16), else with the webview's Web Speech API where it has one.
  - the system share sheet for an image the webview drew (`share_image`: Flock Wrapped's poster, §3.19), since Android's WebView has neither downloads nor Web Share;
  - **Share to TeleFlock**: the app manifest takes `ACTION_SEND` (`text/plain`, `image/*`) and `ACTION_SEND_MULTIPLE` (`image/*`); the plugin's `Share.kt` reads text, subject and up to 8 images while the sender's URI grant lasts, keeps them until the page takes them (`take_shares`, announced by a `share` plugin event, also read at start for a cold launch), and Dispatch opens prefilled (§6.4);
  - the **preview window** (`open_preview`, `PreviewActivity.kt`): a preview tunnel's page (`http://127.0.0.1:<port>/`, loopback only) in an activity of its own whose WebView is a plain one (no `ipc` bridge object, no document-start scripts, no request interception), with Close, Reload and Open in browser. Loopback pages stay in it; other http(s) links open in the browser on a tap (a page redirecting by itself stays put); other schemes go nowhere (`PreviewLinks.kt`); downloads go to the browser; camera, microphone and location requests are denied; an upload field opens the system file picker. The app's page never frames a tunnel (§8, "Framed content").
- **Quick capture (desktop, `src/capture.rs`).** A second webview window (label `capture`, `capture.html`): small, undecorated, always on top, on every workspace, out of the taskbar, created on first use. A system-wide shortcut (`tauri-plugin-global-shortcut`; `Settings.captureShortcut`, absent or empty = off until the user sets one in Settings, which suggests `Ctrl+Alt+Space` / macOS `Cmd+Shift+Space`; the tray item and `teleflock://capture` need no shortcut), the tray's **Quick capture…** and `teleflock://capture` open it. Its page never starts the core: it exchanges webview events with the main window, which owns the engine subscription and the stores and does the work (§6.4). `on_page_load` resets the engine's page state (terminals, sessions) for the `main` webview only. Linux registers on X11 only: under Wayland no app can grab keys system-wide, so Settings says so and offers `<exe> teleflock://capture` for the desktop's own shortcut settings; the tray item works everywhere. Registration failures are reported, not retried (`capture_status`: taken / invalid / failed).
- **Builds.** Windows NSIS via cargo-xwin; signed APK (`docs/build.md`). `scripts/build-hostd.sh` builds tf-hostd for `x86_64/aarch64-unknown-linux-musl` (zigbuild) and `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` before the app.

## 6. Frontend

### 6.1 Core (`src/core`, owner: FrontCore)
**Transports** (`CoreTransport`):
```ts
interface CoreTransport {
  call(hostId: string | null, method: string, params: unknown): Promise<unknown>;
  termInput(hostId: string, termId: string, data: string): void;
  subscribe(onEvent: (e: AppEvent) => void): () => void;
}
```
- `tauri`: invoke + Channel.
- `ws`: devserver. Its URL comes from `?core=ws://…` or the same origin `/ws` when served by the devserver.
- `demo`: in-process TS simulator, `src/core/demo/`.
- Selection:
  - Inside Tauri, use tauri.
  - When served by the devserver, use ws.
  - With `?demo` or when no backend is reachable, use demo.
  - The demo host is also a selectable host kind (`demo`) inside the real app: demo profiles are routed to the demo engine.

**Typed client:**
```ts
core.app<M extends keyof AppMethods>(method: M, params: AppMethods[M]['params']): Promise<AppMethods[M]['result']>
core.host(hostId)<M extends keyof HostMethods>(method: M, params: HostMethods[M]['params']): Promise<HostMethods[M]['result']>
```
Errors reject with `ApiError`.

**Event pipeline.**
- Events are queued and flushed once per animation frame into zustand (`unstable_batchedUpdates` is not needed with React 19).
- Pulses and terminal frames bypass React: they go to imperative subscriber registries (`pulseBus`, `termBus`).

**Stores and hooks.** Fine-grained selectors only. Agent keys are `${hostId}::${agentId}`.
- `useHosts()`: profiles, statuses, active filter.
- `useHostState(hostId)`.
- `useAgent(key)`.
- `useAgentKeys(filter)`.
- `useColumnKeys(column, filter)`.
- `useWorkspaces(hostId)`.
- `useSession(key)`:
  - meta, `ui`, ordered item ids;
  - `useSessionItem(key, itemId)`: per-row subscription;
  - `openSession(key)` and `closeSession(key)` are ref-counted.
- `useInbox()`: needs items across hosts, oldest first.
- `useNotifications()`, `useActivity(hostId)`, `useSettings()`, `useTunnels()`, `useForwards()` (forwarding rules, status and streams; `null` until `forward.list` answers).
- `useAccountLimits(hostIds, everyMs)` (`limits.ts`): one shared `host.limits` poller per host; the shortest cadence among its subscribers wins and `null` subscribes without polling. The board headline and a session's inspector ask every 5 min (`LIMITS_EVERY_MS`, omp's own cache), the Usage page every minute (`LIMITS_USAGE_EVERY_MS`) plus its refresh button; all pass `null` while the window is inactive. A host whose last answer is older than the cadence is asked as soon as a subscriber polls again (window back in front, Usage opened, host back online); a remount within the cadence reuses the cached answer instead of running `omp usage` again.
- `pulseBus.subscribe(key, cb)`.
- `termBus.subscribe(hostId, termId, cb)`.
- `useUi()`: navigation (view, selected agent, split, palette, sheets, mobile tab, pager).
- `@/core/clipboardImages`: `imageFiles(clipboardData | dataTransfer)` reads the files of `items` (WebKitGTK puts pasted screenshots only there), or of `files` when `items` lists none, never both (Chromium lists a pasted image in both as two File objects with different `lastModified`, so merging attached it twice); `attachImages(draftKey, files)` scales images over 3 MB down to ≤ 2560 px (`shrinkImage`) and adds them to the draft within one prompt's caps (`fitImages`: 8 images, `MAX_PROMPT_IMAGE_CHARS` = 12 MiB of base64 under the relay's 16 MiB message cap, checked against the images attached by then). Used by the session composer and Dispatch, which also refuse a send above the cap.
- `@/core/workload`: per-host load for Dispatch Auto (`hostLoad`, `rankHosts`, `pickHost`, `describeLoad`, `explainPick`): running agents and dispatches on their way, needs-you agents, CPU pressure (max of CPU% and load/cores), memory above 85 %, and the chosen model's quota on that host (`providerQuota`: from its `host.limits`, the binding window of the provider's account with the most room; used share ×3, unknown half, exhausted +50); offline hosts never picked. The entry's `placed` says why ("most Claude 5 Hour left of 2: …" when the quota changed the pick).

**Parked and pruned agents** (`store/lifecycle.ts` pure, `agentLifecycle.ts` effects):
- Mark done sends `agent.archive {archived: true, close: Settings.closeDone}`; the Undo toast unarchives (inside the host's 10 s window that just cancels the close, after it the host resumes the parked agent). Older hosts ignore `close`.
- A parked agent (`Agent.parked`: column done, no pane/tab/workspace, same id) shows a quiet "closed" chip on the board and in Dispatch, and has no workspace in the sidebar tree. Resume (`resumeAgent(key)` → `agent.resume`), Reopen (unarchive) and a prompt (`agent.prompt`) all resume it; Terminal resumes first, then opens the new pane. While it resumes, `useResuming(key)` says "Resuming…". With `parked.worktree` (hosts ≥ 0.5.2) its card checks that worktree's pull request and, once it is merged or closed, offers Delete worktree by path (`worktree.remove {workspaceId: "", path}`); the card stays.
- Following a resume: the resumed agent has a new id and the same omp session. The reducer links old key → new key in `CoreState.successors`, from `Started.agentId` (`$successor`) or by matching `omp.sessionId`/`sessionFile` against a parked agent that left the list within 30 s (either patch order, or the new agent first). Once the new agent is in host state the core redirects the route (agent and terminal, `replace`), the split and the board cursor; `onAgentMoved` listeners move the unsent drafts (text and images) and the Terminal tab's memory. `useSuccessor(key)` resolves the chain for anything else.
- Session view on a parked agent: `session.open` may return a read-only snapshot (caps all false except `prompt`): the transcript shows under a "Done and closed" strip with Resume; if it fails (`parked`), a notice with Resume replaces the transcript. The composer stays usable either way: sending resumes the agent with the prompt. The terminal view of a parked agent shows the same notice.
- Pruned (`HostInfo.pruned` gained entries, earlier ids before resumes included): the reducer drops the agents' notifications and session caches and ignores late `notify`s about them; `onAgentsPruned` listeners (replayed to late registrants, idempotent) drop drafts and draft images, the mute entry, toasts, Dispatch entries (`pruneAgents`, by agent id or the `sessionId` captured while live) and the Terminal tab's memory; a route, split or board cursor on the agent goes back to the board.
- Older hosts: no `parked`, `agent.resume` → `unknown_method` (Resume is only offered on parked agents; the toast says to update the host).

**Demo host.** A rich live simulation of the draft data:
- 10 agents across 4 repos, every column populated;
- streaming tokens around 40 tok/s with thinking;
- tool calls including diffs and bash output;
- asks and approvals that can be answered;
- todos progressing, subagents, a cost ticker, health jitter;
- fake terminal frames (ANSI);
- done agents' lifecycle (`demo/parking.ts`): Mark done with close parks an idle agent after 10 s (a worktree agent keeps `parked.worktree`, and its PR stays known by path), resume/prompt/unarchive brings it back under a new id with the same session, `tokens` and `gobump` (merged PR, Delete worktree by path) are seeded parked, and `spike` (seeded parked) is pruned 60 s after start; ended cards (§3.25): `soak` (herdr stopped mid-turn, no ticket until herdr runs again), `a11y` (a countdown that brings it back by itself), `invoices` (a cut-off reply, manual: auto-resume off).

**Delivery that cannot freeze a transcript** (`buses.ts`, `client.ts` `ingest`, `transport/{tauri,ws}.ts`, `store/reduceSafely.ts`, `failures.ts`, `sessionHeal.ts`). A listener that throws (a terminal, pulse or raw `onAppEvent` callback, a transport listener) costs nothing else: each call is isolated, the event still reaches every other listener and the store, and the rest of a Tauri batch is delivered. A frame whose one-pass reduction throws is reduced again event by event, skipping only the events that throw. Both log at most once per call site per 10 s with a count. An open transcript that disagrees with host state (the agent's card in review or done, `omp.link` live, no tool, stream or thought live, nothing needing the user, the host online) while its newest 64 rows, after the last one that ended a run (`stopReason` stop, length, aborted or error), show a pending or running tool or a streaming reply or thought, for 30 s, and that heard no `session` event for 30 s, is re-opened (`session.open`, answered from the app core's mirror; a failed re-open keeps the rows), with a warning; the next waits 30 s, doubling up to 15 min (`bun test src/core/sessionHeal.test.ts src/core/client.test.ts`).

It must exercise every UI state, including disconnected, stale and auth-required on a second demo host.

### 6.2 Feature ownership

| Agent | Owns | Scope |
|---|---|---|
| FrontShell | `src/app/**`, `src/features/{shell,hosts,settings,palette,notify}/**` | Desktop chrome and layout (title bar with host pill and global heartbeat, sidebar tree, view routing, split view frame, ambient glow), command palette, notification center and toasts, hosts manager and connect flow (Tailscale auth-required card, diagnostics), host setup checklist, settings, keyboard map and shortcuts sheet, mobile shell (bottom nav, sheets host, gestures) |
| FrontBoard | `src/features/{board,inbox,activity,usage}/**` | Bento summary row, kanban (dnd-kit, card flights, liveness cards, quick answers, filters, J/K), mobile Flock home, inbox and answer-next HUD (Ctrl+J), activity feed with "while you were away", usage dashboard |
| FrontSession | `src/features/session/**` | Session view on desktop and mobile: virtualized transcript, streaming markdown with highlighting, tool cards, ask/approval/plan-review cards, composer (slash palette, @files, images, steer/follow-up queue), model/thinking/plan/goal/fast controls, inspector (context, cost, tps, todos, subagents, files, tree, actions), btw, non-omp terminal-first view, mobile agent pager |
| FrontTerminal | `src/features/terminal/**` | xterm.js WebGL terminal (frames, input, resize, scroll), tabs, send-keys bar, mobile extra keys (sticky Ctrl/Alt), images/videos pasted, dropped or picked → uploaded (`upload.*`) and their host paths pasted as one bracketed paste (omp attaches them; `src/features/terminal/README.md`), embeddable `<TerminalPane>` for split view and session |
| FrontOps | `src/features/{worktree,workspace,review,preview,history}/**` | New worktree/agent wizard with remote folder browser, workspace ops, review and ship (git diff viewer, commit/push/PR/merge), preview tunnels, past sessions and resume |
| Dispatch | `src/features/dispatch/**` | Dispatch screen: per-repo chat thread where each message creates a worktree + omp agent, with live reply cards; images; Auto mode over a host group |
| Capture | `src/features/capture/**`, `src/capture/**`, `capture.html` | Prefilled tasks from outside the composers: the Quick capture window (desktop) and its main-window bridge, Android share intake, the Dispatch prefill banner with Send to agent…, Settings → Quick capture (§6.4) |
| Rambler | `src/core/rambler.ts`, `src/features/dictation/{useRamblerDictation.tsx,RamblerCard.tsx,edit.ts}`, `src/features/settings/RamblerSettings.tsx` | Dictation tidied into a prompt by a model on the host: the controller, the card and strip above the composers, the Settings rows (§3.8, §6.10) |
| Dictation | `src/features/dictation/{DictationSettings.tsx,deviceSession.ts,models.ts,store.ts}`, `src/core/demo/dictation.ts` | On-device dictation in the app: Settings → Dictation (models, downloads, language, microphone), the app core's engine as the composers' mic (`useDictation`), the demo's engine (§6.16) |
| OmpSettings | `src/features/ompSettings/**` | Per-host omp settings page: omp's tabs/groups/labels, search, per-type controls, model-roles editor, Other tab for keys omp's panel lacks (§3.10) |
| AgentRoles | `src/features/agentRoles/**` | One agent's own model roles and fallback chains: the editor in New agent / New worktree / Dispatch / Fan-out / Automations (shared Agent options area) and the running session's "Roles (this session)" dialog; the fallback-chain editor shared with the omp settings page (§3.7, §3.10) |
| RefusalUI | `src/features/refusal/**`, `src/core/refusal.ts`, `src/features/session/transcript/RefusalActions.tsx` | Refusal fallback in the app: Settings → Refusal fallback (per-host rules, the form, the Opus → Sonnet suggestion), "Retry with" and "Always do this" on the refusal row, the link under omp's fallback chains, the shared matching and normalising of tf-hostd's rules (§3.22) |
| Automations | `src/features/automations/**` | Per-host Automations page: list with next/last run, editor with schedule builder and live preview, run history, scheduler status (§3.11) |
| HostSync | `src/features/hostSync/**` | "Make this host like another": the dialog (source pick, plan by section with per-item choice, secrets confirm step, apply, results), its entry points (omp area strip, host menu, palette) and the pure model + call sequence (§3.24) |
| Janitor | `src/features/cleanup/**` | Per-host Cleanup page: worktrees by pull request, age, changes, size and last agent; Delete merged/closed, Prune stale; omp storage check/apply; the board's low-disk notice (§3.17) |
| Forwarding | `src/features/forward/**` | The Forwarding page: rules with status, active streams with Stop, the rule editor, the commands and agent instruction to copy for each rule (§6.8) |
| Wall | `src/features/{wall,ambient}/**` | Wall: 2×2 / 3×3 grid of live session tiles with auto-fill, pins, drag-to-swap, in-tile answers and one composer on the active tile; Ambient mode: one orb per agent for a tablet or second screen, tap to answer, optional per-state chimes, wake lock (§6.3) |
| FrontCore | `src/core/**` (except `gen/`) | §6.1 |

Shared UI building blocks come from `@/design` only. A feature that needs a cross-cutting primitive asks FrontShell (for layout) or adds it in its own folder.

Routes (hash router, `src/app/router.ts`):
- `#/board` and `#/` (home)
- `#/dispatch`
- `#/agent/:hostId/:agentId` (session)
- `#/terminal/:hostId[/:termTarget]`
- `#/review/:hostId/:workspaceId`
- `#/activity`
- `#/usage`
- `#/wrapped` (Flock Wrapped: the week's card, §3.19)

- `#/automations[/:hostId]` (automations of a host)
- `#/cleanup[/:hostId]` (worktrees and omp storage of a host)
- `#/forward` (host-to-host forwarding, a device-level page: no host in the route, §6.8)
- `#/wall` (desktop; phones get a pointer to ambient mode)
- `#/ambient` (bare: the shell hides the sidebar, split and bottom nav, `isBare` in `src/app/routes.tsx`)
- `#/design`

On mobile, the same routes render mobile layouts (`useIsMobile`).

### 6.3 Rendering budget (design system, `src/design`)
An open, idle window must cost next to nothing: WebView2 on iGPU laptops and WebKitGTK at fractional scale pay for every composited frame and every translucent pixel.
- **Motion levels** (`html[data-motion]`, `MotionProvider`, `motion.css`). Calm (default) holds the screen still: no drifting ambient glow, no running-state loops, shimmer text ("Thinking…", "reconnecting") stands still; attention loops (needs-you breath/ping/sunset pulse/glow border, tool ring) play three cycles when they appear and end on the element's base style, so no finished animation keeps a compositor layer. Values that change jump in Calm and glide only in Lively with the window active: sparkline paths (`.tf-spark-path`, a main-thread CSS `d` transition), proportional bars (`.tf-glide-width`/`.tf-glide-grow`: lane bar, RAM bars; CSS transitions, no JS springs) and the todo ring's glow filter (`.tf-ring__arc`). Lively loops everything and drifts the ambient blooms; Reduced animates nothing. `useMotionLevel()` returns the level; `useCalmMotion()` means *reduced*.
- **Inactive window** (`lib/windowActive.ts`: window blurred or Tauri `onFocusChanged` false, or hidden): `html[data-active="false"]` pauses every loop in place (`tf-keep` spinners excepted), motion's transform and layout animations jump to their end (`MotionConfig reducedMotion="always"`), heartbeats stop scrolling and limits polling pauses (§6.1). **Hidden window** (document hidden, or in the desktop app the native window minimized or hidden to the tray: WebView2 keeps a visible document then, so `isVisible`/`isMinimized` are asked every 2 s while the window has no focus): additionally the shared clocks hold still (time texts, `TpsSparkline` and inspector throughput sampling freeze; every clock ticks once when the window is shown again) and the board's live terminal tile detaches (no attach, no xterm work). Clocks and the tile keep running while merely unfocused, so a board watched on a second monitor stays truthful.
- **Surfaces.** `glass` is a translucent tint without `backdrop-filter` (sidebar, session, inspector, composer, split view; mobile header/nav are dense tints): over the soft ambient glow a blur is invisible but re-blurs its backdrop whenever anything inside or behind changes. `glass-blur` keeps the blur for small transient overlays above sharp content (selection bar, jump-to-latest pill); tooltips, popovers, toasts and dialog scrims keep theirs.
- **Layers.** No permanent `will-change`; `TiltCard` carries a transform only while hovered. An idle Calm board is ~17 compositor layers (75 before 0.5.0).
- **Layout measurement.** Every motion `layout` element names what moves it in `layoutDependency`, so a re-render for data reads no layout (without it motion snapshots on every render and the root re-measures scroll inside the commit): sidebar agent rows their index, Activity rows their index in the day, Dispatch items their index, notifications their index and resolved/answered state, the session view's columns focus mode and the inspector rail. Board cards (`CardFlight`) sit in a `FlightGroup` whose `arrangement` is every lane's keys in order: any change re-measures the whole group at once, which is what lets a card fly between lanes and its neighbours slide.
- **Per-second work.** One shared clock per period (`useNow`, `null` = not subscribed); components needing it are leaves (`Elapsed`, heartbeat age, `QuietChip`); `useQuiet` is a threshold timer; `useActivity` ticks only while it has recent beats; `TpsSparkline` stops on a flat zero line. Heartbeats scroll at frame rate only in Lively; Calm, Reduced, an inactive window or an off-screen trace step the trace once per beat (≤ 2.8 Hz per agent) and flatline in one step ~5 s after the last one. In Calm the title bar's flock heartbeat takes at most one beat per second (`liveness.ts`). A trace narrower than 40 px (a squeezed board card) has no canvas.
- **React Compiler.** Components are compiled (`babel-plugin-react-compiler` through `@rolldown/plugin-babel`), which requires `@babel/core` 7: under Babel 8 every destructuring default bailed out silently (86 functions in 0.5.0). `vite build` compiles a canary of everyday component syntax first and fails when the compiler skips any of it; components that bail out for reasons of their own (try/finally, refs read in render, eslint suppressions; 45 functions) still build, and the build log counts them (`react-compiler: N functions compiled, M skipped`; `TF_COMPILER_REPORT=1` lists them).
- **Inspector cards.** Every inspector card collapses (chevron or title click; remembered per card in `tf.inspector`, with the side questions the user dismissed or collapsed per session) and a collapsed card unmounts its body for a one-line summary, so a collapsed tree or throughput chart costs nothing; the rail is its own scroller, so the virtualized transcript's width and height do not change. The Subagents card (desktop rail and the phone's Agents tab) is a tree (`subagents/grouping.ts` `groupSubagents`): each subagent's own subagents hang under it at any depth (`Subagent.parentId`, else omp's dotted id, `A.B.C` under `A.B`; one whose parent the roster lacks stays at the top with "from <id>"), on a rail behind a roll-up line ("3 subagents · 2 running · 1 failed") that folds them. A branch is open while anything below it runs or failed; the user's choice sticks per session on this device (`tf.inspector` `subs`, the newest 300 ids of the newest 40 sessions). Each level orders running, then failed (a branch counts as either when anything in it does), then finished (latest end first); a finished parent with a running or failed descendant stays out of the collapsed "N completed" group, and the header counts are totals at every depth. Folded branches and the completed group render no rows; building the tree is linear plus a sort per level (0.9 ms for 680 subagents). The subagent viewer steps `[` / `]` through the same order and names a nested subagent's parents. See `src/features/session/README.md`.
- **Review Markdown.** Rendered view of a Markdown file shows the document in chunks of whole blocks inside the viewer's virtualizer (measured rows, only chunks in view render); files over 400 KB stay a diff.
- **Session tree.** The inspector's tree renders only the rows in view (fixed 24 px rows, 30 px on touch) and lays the tree out again only when its leaf or node count changes: hostd re-sends the whole tree with every session meta.
- **Wall and ambient mode** (`src/features/{wall,ambient}`). A wall tile is a compact `SessionPane` with the composer only on the active tile and no blurred sunset bloom behind ask cards (`bloom={false}`); non-omp agents get the board tile's observe-mode DOM terminal (no PTY resize, no WebGL context); tiles re-render on lane changes, not per token; while the window is hidden the tiles unmount their transcripts, releasing the nine sessions. Ambient mode opens no session: static radial-gradient glows (no blur), attention layers as `tf-loop` classes (three cycles in Calm, again on the 20 s nudge), the working sheen and thinking pulse only in Lively, times every 15 s. Measured in headless Chromium (production build, `?demo`, Calm, focused, 1920×1080, 20 s after 8 s of settling, on a loaded 2026 build machine; the demo's simulated hosts stream tokens the whole time, so nothing is idle): wall 3×3 74% main-thread task time (61 long tasks), board 42% (67), ambient 6% (9, 0.5 s in total).
- **Terminal.** WebGL for full panes (board tiles stay on DOM), context released explicitly on dispose (`WEBGL_lose_context`); xterm pauses cursor blink while unfocused.
- **Windows/Linux windows.** The Windows window is opaque (`transparent: false`; nothing uses Mica, and a transparent WebView2 window is alpha-composited every frame). On Linux no WebKitGTK env var is set by default; `WEBKIT_DISABLE_DMABUF_RENDERER=1` (blank or flickering window on some NVIDIA/Wayland stacks) and `WEBKIT_DISABLE_COMPOSITING_MODE=1` are user opt-ins, see `docs/build.md`.
- **Tray and taskbar (shell).** While agents run, only Lively orbits the tray comet (an icon swap every 110 ms: rasterize, `CreateIcon`, `Shell_NotifyIconW`, an explorer redraw, ~9 per second for as long as anything runs) and shows the taskbar's indeterminate marquee. Calm and Reduced keep a still comet and a still full progress bar (paused yellow while something needs you, in every level); Linux never animates the tray (`src-tauri/src/tray.rs`).

### 6.4 Prefilled tasks: Quick capture and Share to TeleFlock (`src/features/capture`)
One entry point, `openPrefilledTask(task, place?)`, puts a task that started outside the app's composers into Dispatch: it picks the thread (`prefillPlace`: a shared GitHub issue or pull request link prefers a host with a clone of that repository, matched like Auto by canonical remote; otherwise Dispatch's current target and last repo; with no repo picked the task waits for the first pick), appends the text to that thread's composer draft and attaches the images through `attachImages` (scaled and capped like a paste), then shows Dispatch with a banner whose **Send to agent…** moves exactly what the task added into an existing agent's composer instead.
- **Android share** (`androidShare.ts`): each share the plugin read (§5.4) becomes `composeShared({text, subject})` (the page title leads, a bare issue link gets a "GitHub issue o/r#12" line) plus its images.
- **Quick capture** (desktop): the capture page (`CaptureWindow`) holds the typed task and pasted screenshots and asks the main window over webview events (`link.ts`; a same-origin BroadcastChannel outside Tauri, which is how the headless checks drive it): `send` (Dispatch it now through `features/dispatch/send.ts`, the same placement and checks as the Dispatch composer: Auto, hostd 0.5 for images, base and name resolved on the host), `edit` (`openPrefilledTask`, main window to the front), `answer` (`agent.answer` with one option of the oldest needs-you request; number keys, single choice only) and `open` (the Answer-next HUD). `QuickCaptureBridge` (mounted once in `App`) answers each with `done` and, only while the window shows (hello / bye), posts a `CaptureSnapshot` (target, recent threads, up to 9 questions) whenever it changes; an idle hidden capture window costs nothing.

### 6.5 Large pastes as attachments (`src/core/pastes.ts`, `src/core/draftPastes.ts`, `features/session/composer/usePasteAttach.ts`)
The session and Dispatch composers take a large text paste the way omp's own editor does (omp 18.13.3 pi-tui `editor.ts` #handlePaste, coding-agent `input-controller.ts` handleLargePaste / presentLargePasteMenu). A paste of more than 10 lines or 1000 characters (CRLF → LF first; up to 4 MiB of UTF-8, else refused with a toast) goes in as omp's marker `[Paste #N, +L lines]` / `[Paste #N, C chars]` plus a space, as one undoable textarea edit, and shows as a chip above the text (first line, `#N`, size, how it goes). From the host's `paste.largeMenuThreshold` lines on (read with `omp.settingValues`, §3.10, per host for 2 min; older hosts and failures: omp's default 100; `0` never) omp's menu opens with omp's three choices and words: **Attach as a wrapped block** (the paste is sent as `<attachment>\n…\n</attachment>`), **Attach as local file** (`session.writeLocal`, §3.20: the marker becomes `local://paste-N.md`; failures leave the paste in place with a toast) and **Paste inline**; Esc, typing on or clicking away keeps it inline, like omp's cancel. A chip opens the same menu with the current choice checked plus **Edit as text** (the marker becomes its text) and **Remove**. Markers edit as one unit: Backspace/Delete take a whole marker, the caret steps over one, copy and cut carry the text a marker stands for, and any other edit that cuts into one (word deletion, typing over a selection, IME and phone keyboard corrections) takes all of it (`reconcileTokens`). Sending expands every marker in one pass and trims, as omp does on submit (`expandPastes`; a pasted text holding another marker stays verbatim), so the agent gets exactly what omp's TUI sends; a send waits while a paste is being saved, and refuses a marker whose paste this device lost (a warning chip removes it). Pastes persist with the draft like its images (IndexedDB `tf.draftPastes`, `draftSlots.ts` shared with the images; ≤ 16 per draft, the 3 newest deleted ones kept for undo), follow a resumed agent and go with a pruned one (`draftSweep.ts`). Dispatch has no "local file" (shown off with the reason): its agent's session, where `local://` lives, only exists once the task started it.

### 6.6 herdr health in the app (`HostInfo.herdrHealth`; `src/features/hosts/{herdrHealth.ts,HerdrControls.tsx,HerdrNotice.tsx,HerdrPlan.tsx}`)
`herdrHealth` (§3.29; absent from hosts older than 0.7.0, which hide every control) is the app's one source for "is this host's herdr running": `herdrHealth.ts` turns it into words (headline "herdr stopped at 13:58 · 9 agents ended", status "Starting it again in 12s", or, with `restartHeld`, "herdr kept stopping soon after each start, so TeleFlock stopped restarting it by itself. Restart herdr starts it."; update plan and outcome), `useHerdrDown(hostId)` is the hook everything that shows agents of that host reads. While the server is `down`, `starting`, `restarting` or `unresponsive`: the board tray has a `herdr` notice per host (Restart herdr; forced with a confirm when `unresponsive`), agent cards, the session header and phone pager stop saying Running and stop their timers, `LinkGate` says the herdr reason instead of the collab-room wording, the composer says "herdr isn't running on forge", and the terminal shows a friendly state instead of the raw socket error and reattaches by itself when `epoch` changes. The Hosts card and Tools card show the state line, the update plan (`update`: blockers, countdown to `dueAt`, outcome; state `ready`, for a herdr that can only be loaded by a restart: "herdr update ready · Restart herdr to load 0.9.6", and the button reads Restart herdr), **Load now** behind a confirm that says per `mode` whether panes keep running, and the per-host settings (`host.herdrConfig`: restart when it stops, load updates when idle after N minutes; under them a warning line when `configEdited` says TeleFlock keeps an edited managed herdr config and no longer copies the user's into it).

### 6.7 Which host a session is on (`src/features/hosts/HostBadge.tsx`)
`HostBadge` is the one chip that names the host of an agent or session: the host's `hostAccent` swatch and name, plus a status orb while the host is not online. It renders only with more than one saved host (`useMultiHost`), like the board's cards and Wall tiles; a lone host is the whole app, so its name is not repeated. It appears in the desktop session header (the repo/title line, shrinking with the title beside it, 9rem at most, dropped in a very narrow identity column), the Focus header, the non-omp session view, the split pane's header (terminal and second session; the terminal-without-agent title drops its `· host` suffix while the chip is there), the full Terminal screen's heading, the phone top bar's subtitle (capped at 45% of the width, the repo and branch take the rest) and the subagent viewer's meta row. Hovering with a mouse (after 280 ms) or clicking/tapping opens a popover: connection state with latency, connection kind (Tailscale, SSH, Local, WSL, Demo) and address (`profileAddress`), hostname and OS from `HostInfo`, and **Open hosts** / **Edit** (`hostEditor` dialog). Opened by hover or pointer it leaves focus where it was (a composer keeps it), and a click on a chip the hover opened keeps it open; opened with Enter or Space it moves focus to its first button (Tab cycles its buttons, Esc closes it and focus returns to the chip). The phone pager list and the "Next:" chip over the composer, and in-app needs-you/finished toasts, put the host name in their text while there are several hosts; where that text is cut short (one row in the pager list, two lines in a phone toast, whose title and text each wrap to two lines at most), a long user-chosen name never hides the rest: in the pager list and phone toasts the name takes at most 40% of the row with its own ellipsis (`HostName`), and "Next:" names the ask and how many more wait first and gives the host the room left; OS toasts (`present.rs`), the bell list, inbox rows and the answer HUD (`NeedsHeader`) already carried it.

### 6.8 Forwarding (`forward.*`, `AppEvent::Forwards`; `src/features/forward`, `src/core/forward.ts`, `src/core/demo/forward.ts`)
`#/forward` is the page for host-to-host forwarding (the core is a device-level feature: rules live on this device, so the route has no host). Reached from Hosts (a Forwarding button beside Add host once two hosts exist), Settings → Forwarding, phone More, and the palette.
- **Page** (`ForwardView`): one `RuleCard` per rule, then **In use now** (`Streams`). A card shows `from → to` with connection dots, the `tf-forward <name>` chip, the status chip (`statusLook`: Ready, Waiting, Update needed, Off, Error) and the core's message, what the rule offers (Shell, tools, `:listen → :target`), each `portErrors` row, an on/off switch (saves the rule again with `enabled` flipped) and Edit/Delete (Delete toasts with Undo, which saves the rule again as a new one). *Waiting* offers Open Hosts; *Update needed* offers Reconnect for each host whose `HostInfo.hostdVersion` is older than 0.8.0 (it redeploys when the app reconnects). A stream row shows its kind, label, duration, bytes each way and Stop (`forward.stop`; the rule stays). With fewer than two hosts the page explains that forwarding needs two and offers Add host. Android adds a note that forwards only work while the app runs connected to both hosts (Stay connected in the background).
- **Use it** (`Commands`, `model.ts`): per card, exactly what to copy for the from-host: `tf-forward <name> -- <command>`, `tf-forward <name>`, the PATH line for shells started by hand whenever the rule has a shell or tools (`tf-forward` sits with the shims; `export PATH="$HOME/.teleflock/forward/bin:$PATH"`; `set "PATH=%USERPROFILE%\.teleflock\forward\bin;%PATH%"` for a Windows from-host; with a shell its note offers `tf-forward`'s full path `~/.teleflock/forward/bin/tf-forward` instead on a Unix from-host; with tools it adds that a `~/.bashrc` setting PATH outright hides them from omp's bash tool on a Unix from-host unless the line comes right after that assignment, and where a window a tool opens appears: on Linux in the desktop session the user is logged in to, on macOS and Windows only with a tf-hostd the app on that computer started, §3.30), per port what `127.0.0.1:<listen>` reaches with an example (`agent-browser --cdp 9222`, else `curl`), and a one-paragraph instruction to hand to an agent that names only what the rule offers. On a Unix from-host the instruction names `tf-forward` by its path (`~/.teleflock/forward/bin/tf-forward <name> -- <command>`) and gives each tool's path next to its plain name, so it holds whatever PATH the agent's shell ends up with; a Windows from-host keeps the plain names (omp reads no rc there).
- **Editor** (`RuleEditor`, a dialog on desktop and a sheet on phones): from and to host, name (follows the to-host's label as `slugify(label)`, numbered when taken on the from-host, until typed over), Shell access, tool chips (suggests `agent-browser`), port pairs (a new first pair suggests the first free listen port from 9222), On. The security warning (everything on the from host, its agents included, can then act as you on the to host) sits at the top of every editor, so it is read before the first save. `ruleErrors` (`src/core/forward.ts`) shows every problem at once with the core's messages; an `invalid_params` from `forward.save` is mapped by its `fromHost:|toHost:|name:|shell:|tools:|ports:` prefix to the same field (`parseFieldError`).
- **Store**: `forwards: ForwardList | null` in the core state, read with `useForwards()`; `forward.list` is asked at bootstrap, `AppEvent::Forwards` replaces the whole list. Demo-kind hosts inside a real app are not offered (the real core does not know them).
- **Demo host** (`DemoForwards`): answers `forward.*` with the same validation messages over the demo hosts: two seeded rules (`forge → studio-pc` with a shell, `agent-browser` and port 9222; `studio-pc → forge` with port 5432, which the demo says is taken), status from the hosts' connections (Waiting while one is not online; studio-pc connects only when asked, so Connect it on Hosts), three fake streams that open when the first rule turns ready (counters move every 2 s) and end when it stops being ready, a rule's streams ending when saving it removes what they used, and rules dropped with their host.

### 6.9 Themes (design system, `src/design/theme`; Settings → Appearance in `src/features/settings/{Appearance,ThemeEditor,ThemePreview}.tsx`)
`src/design/styles/tokens.css` is the default theme and the only place colours are defined. A theme is plain CSS that overrides those variables on `:root`; it never adds a second token system. TeleFlock puts the applied theme in its own unlayered `<style id="tf-theme">`, always the last element of `<head>`: Tailwind emits the defaults in `@layer theme`/`base`, so the theme wins whatever order styles load in. `html[data-theme]` names it.
- **Variables a theme may set** (`vars.ts` `THEME_VAR_GROUPS`, the editor's reference and every template list them with a hint; `@theme static` emits all of them, used or not). Plus `color-scheme: light|dark` on `:root`.
  - Surfaces: `--color-bg`, `-bg-deep`, `-chrome`, `-tile-1`…`-tile-5`, `-well`, `-well-2`, `-glass` (translucent panels at 72 %), `-pop-1`/`-pop-2` (menus, popovers, selects, command palette), `-sheet-1`/`-sheet-2` (dialogs, bottom sheets), `-float` (tooltips, toasts at 94–98 %), `-scrim`.
  - Text and lines: `--color-ink` (also the base of every neutral wash), `-ink-2`, `-mute`, `-mute-2`, `-line`, `-line-2`, `-line-3`.
  - Accent: `--color-accent`, `-accent-hi`, `-accent-lo`, `-accent-ink` (text on accent tints), `-on-accent` (text on accent fills), `-omp-pink`/`-omp-purple`/`-omp-blue` (the omp gradient).
  - Status: `--color-run-1`/`-run-2`/`-run-solid`/`-on-run`, `-sun-1`/`-sun-2`/`-on-sun`/`-sun-ink` (needs you), `-review`/`-review-2`/`-on-review`, `-done`/`-done-2`/`-on-done`, `-idle`/`-idle-1`/`-idle-2`/`-on-idle`, `-error`/`-error-2`/`-on-error`/`-error-ink`, `-warn`/`-warn-ink`. `[data-status]` derives `--st-1`, `--st-2`, `--st-solid`, `--st-ink`, `--st-grad`, `--st-glow` from them (`--st-rgb` is gone: alpha is `color-mix(in srgb, var(--st-solid) N%, transparent)` / `bg-(--st-solid)/N`).
  - Diffs: `--color-add`, `-del`, `-tool`. Depth and texture: `--shadow-d1|d2|d3|key|hairline|glow-*`, `--tf-grad-omp|sun|run` (derived from the colours), `--tf-noise` (`none` turns the grain off).
  - Terminal: `--term-bg`, `-fg`, `-cursor`, `-cursor-ink`, `-selection` and the 16 ANSI colours `--term-black` … `--term-bright-white`; xterm (`features/terminal/theme.ts` `termTheme()`, re-read on every theme change) and ANSI output in tool cards (`lib/ansi.ts`, painted on `--term-bg`) use them.
  - Syntax: `--syntax-keyword|string|number|comment|function|type|operator|punctuation|property|attribute|variable|tag|literal`, shared by transcript code blocks, tool-card diffs and the review diff viewer.
- **Components** use the tokens, never dark-only literals: neutral washes are `bg-ink/N` (a tint of the text colour: lighter on dark themes, darker on light ones); text on fills is `text-on-accent|on-sun|on-error`; `white` is literal (switch knobs, the sunset's own decorations, photos). `card-sunset` / `card-omp` re-map `ink`/`mute`/`line` to the fill's `on-*` colour inside. Canvas, SVG and xterm read variables at runtime (`readThemeVar`, `readThemeColor` / `resolveColor` → `rgba()`, which also resolve `oklch()`, `hsl()` and named colours a theme may use for status tokens, `useThemeVersion`/`onThemeChange`); the Lightbox stays dark in every theme.
- **Premade themes** (`presets.ts`, the same CSS format): TeleFlock (the default, empty CSS), Daylight (light surfaces, deep violet; the terminal and tool output stay dark because omp and most TUIs draw for a dark background), High contrast (black, white outlines, every text ≥ 7:1, the sunset with black text), Nord, Dracula, Catppuccin Mocha, Gruvbox. The sunset keeps white text in the others, so its colours stay saturated. `presets.test.ts` checks each against tokens.css: valid CSS with known variables only, and WCAG contrast of text on the surfaces it sits on (ink ≥ 7:1, body/mute ≥ 4.5:1, status and `*-ink` colours, `on-*` on their fills, terminal and syntax colours on theirs).
- **Custom themes** (Settings → Appearance): **New theme** from any premade theme writes the full template (`themeTemplate`: header `TeleFlock theme: <name>` / `Starts from:`, `color-scheme`, every variable with that theme's value, read from the stylesheet with derivations kept, `readThemeDefaults`); from a custom theme it copies its CSS. The editor checks as you type (`inspectThemeCss`: errors with line numbers, typo suggestions for unknown TeleFlock variables, non-colour values) and previews live in a scoped box (`previewCss`: every variable reset to the default, then the theme's rules rewritten under `[data-theme-preview]` by `scopeThemeCss`, so a broken theme can't reach the page). Import from a `.css` file or pasted text (name from the header, else the file name); export as a `.css` download (not in the Android app, whose WebView has no downloads) or Copy CSS; duplicate; delete with Undo. At most 30 custom themes of 64 KB each.
- **Network and disk**: nothing. `@import` is refused and `url()` (also `image-set()`, `-webkit-image-set()`, `src()`, `image()`) takes `data:` URIs only; a URL function may not take `var()`, `env()`, `attr()`, `if()`, `inherit()` or a custom function (they could hand it an address), a malformed `url()` and a parenthesis left open are errors, and a theme with errors is never saved, previewed or applied. The checks run on the tokens a browser's tokenizer sees (`lexer.ts`, CSS Syntax §4: escapes decoded, so `\75rl(` is `url(` and `@\69mport` is `@import`; `/*` inside a string or an unquoted `url()` is no comment). The desktop CSP would block remote loads anyway (`style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'`, `img-src … data: blob:`, `font-src 'self' data:`); the check makes a plain browser and the devserver behave the same. A theme may use the bundled fonts by family name.
- **Persistence**: per device in localStorage `tf.theme` (`store.ts`: `active`, `activeCss`, `custom`), like `tf.motion`/`tf.prefs`, not app Settings: the theme must apply before the first paint (Settings arrive from the core asynchronously), the Quick capture window reads it without starting the core and follows changes through `storage` events (it never writes), and a devserver shared by several browsers keeps one theme per browser. `main.tsx` and `capture/main.tsx` call `startTheme` before rendering, which also sets `<meta name="theme-color">` and `color-scheme`, and in the desktop app the native window and webview background (`WebviewWindow.setBackgroundColor` with the theme's `--color-bg`; capabilities `core:window:allow-set-background-color` and `core:webview:allow-set-webview-background-color` for `main` and `capture`), so reloads and resizes don't flash the default colour. A window still opens with tauri.conf.json's `backgroundColor` until its page runs; the Android system bars stay dark.
- **Recovery**: a custom theme applied from Appearance runs on trial (`model.ts`): it shows at once with a Keep toast and banner and goes back by itself after 15 s unless kept; trials are never persisted, so a restart during one starts with the theme confirmed before, and what a launch applies is the CSS confirmed with Keep (`activeCss`), not the theme's latest text. Saving the theme that is on screen starts a new trial. Escapes: Settings → Appearance → Reset; Ctrl+Alt+Shift+T (⌘⌥⇧T) anywhere in the main window; `?theme=default` in the address (browser, devserver) resets to the default at start. Custom themes are kept in every case.
- **Rendering budget** (§6.3): a theme adds no animation or blur; changing it is one style recalculation and an xterm palette update. The picker's previews are static.

### 6.10 Rambler: dictation tidied into a prompt (`src/core/rambler.ts`, `src/features/dictation`, `src/features/settings/RamblerSettings.tsx`)

Optional (Settings → Dictation → "Rambler: turn rambling into a prompt", `usePrefs.rambler`, off by default; `ramblerRole`: `tiny` default, `smol`, `default`; device-local). It sits on top of whatever dictation source `useDictation(onFinal, onEnd?)` wraps (Web Speech in browsers and the devserver page, Android's system recognizer, the on-device engine): any source calls `onFinal(text)` once per finished utterance, in order, and `onEnd()` once per listening session after its last `onFinal` (stopped, error or engine gone).

**Controller (`createRambler`).** One per dictation session of one text box: `push(segment)` for each utterance, `finish()` once the source ended, `cancel()`, `state()` / `subscribe()` (`raw`, `clean`, `upToDate`, `thinking`, `error`). It keeps "the rewrite of everything said so far" fresh with incremental, coalesced calls: at most one call in flight; utterances that arrive meanwhile only extend the transcript, and the next call (newest transcript, the previous rewrite attached) starts when the running one returns, at least 1.2 s after the previous live call started and for at most 24 live calls per session (so a long ramble costs a bounded number of model calls). `finish()` ignores the pacing and the cap, makes one final call if the last rewrite does not cover the transcript, and resolves with the rewrite, or with the raw transcript after a failed final call or after 15 s. Only a call started after `finish()` is a final one: a live call still running at the stop that fails counts as a failure like any other, and the final call still runs. An `unavailable` / `unsupported` host stops the session's calls at once, a second failure in a row too; the words are never lost: the result is then the raw transcript with the reason.

**Surfaces (`useRamblerDictation`).** The session composer, Dispatch's composer, the ask card's Other field (session card, Inbox / HUD / board quick answers) and the phone's versions of them; the mic shows on desktop too wherever `dictation.supported`. The host is the composer's agent's (Dispatch: its target host; a surface without one: the first online host); the context is the agent's repository and title (Dispatch: the thread's repository, none for a chat). With Rambler on and a host to ask, a recognised utterance goes to the controller instead of the box and the Rambler card above the box shows the live cleaned prompt (the raw words until the first rewrite, what is being heard in italics, "Tidying…" while a call runs, the raw transcript behind a disclosure); on stop the final text is appended to the box as one edit (`insertText` on a focused box, so Ctrl+Z undoes it; on a phone the box is not focused for it, so the draft is written and the strip's Undo reverts it), and a strip stays with **Use raw transcript** (and back to the tidied one) and **Undo**. A failed or slow rewrite fills the raw transcript and the strip says why. Rambler off, or no host: each phrase is appended as it is recognised, as before. A board or Inbox quick answer's Other field that closes (Esc, an answer, the question going) stops its mic as Stop would, so what was said lands in that field's draft. Quick capture has no dictation and so no Rambler.

**Warm start.** When a session starts (the mic is tapped with Rambler on and a host to ask), the surface sends `ai.prewarm` for the Rambler system prompt and role (`prewarmRamble`, `src/core/ai.ts`; fire and forget, errors ignored, a host answering `unknown_method` is not asked again) so the host's omp is already started and kept while the person speaks (Rambler's second rewrite then qualifies anyway) and the first rewrite costs the model's time only (§3.8 "Warm workers").

**Demo host.** `ai.complete` task `ramble` answers deterministically (`src/core/demo/ai.ts`: fillers dropped, one-word self-corrections applied, spoken paths ("src slash net slash upload dot ts") written out, sentences split where a new request starts) after 400–700 ms (`tiny`), 800–1300 (`smol`) or 1500–2300 (`default`).

### 6.11 Diagrams, math and SVG in Markdown (`src/features/session/markdown`, `tools/{preview.ts,FilePreview.tsx}`)
The transcript's one Markdown renderer (`Markdown`, also used by plan reviews, the artifacts viewer, tool cards, IRC and ask cards) draws four more things. All of it runs on the device; nothing is sent anywhere.
- **Parsing** (`parse.ts`, tested in `parse.test.ts`). Display math is a block: `$$…$$` / `\[…\]` on one line, or a delimiter line, the formula and a closing delimiter, which must come before the next blank line (else the lines stay prose: `$$` is also a shell's PID); a delimiter line still waiting for its formula while the message streams is an open block, shown as code with "writing…". Inline math (`$x$`, `\(x\)`, `$$x$$` mid-line) follows pandoc's rules so money and shell variables stay text: the opening `$` has a non-space after it and no letter or digit before it, the closing one a non-space before it and no digit after it (`$5 and $10`, `$5-$10`), a candidate with prose between the dollars (`$5 and got 5$`) or the shape `$HOME/$USER` is refused, `\$` is a dollar sign, and code spans and fences are never scanned. `![alt](src)` is an inline image node; only `data:image/*` sources are shown (`imageSrc` on `Markdown` may map a path to one, as the artifacts viewer does), anything else shows its alt text.
- **Parse cost** (`parse.ts`). Reading a message takes time linear in its length, whatever it holds: agent text is not trusted to be well-formed, and `[` × 400 000 took 89 s to parse. An opener that never closes (`[`, `![`, `*`, `_`, `$`, `\(`, `<http://`) must not send every later opener of its kind to the end of the text again, so each search for a closer remembers its answer (`Ahead`: a closer found ahead of the new start, or "none from an earlier start", answers the next ask), and plain text is passed over by character code with nothing copied off the front of it per character. The other quadratic or worse scans are plain loops now: a heading's text is cut from its end (`# a` and 5 000 blanks took 18 s), a table's separator row and a thematic break are scanned, a display-math delimiter that met no closer before a blank line (or the end) leaves that line behind for the delimiters after it, and a line holding U+2028 is an ordinary line (`.` refused it, and the fence and list-item patterns then tried every way to split the line). Nesting is bounded: quotes and list items inside one another are read 16 levels deep (`> > > …` recursed once per marker, to a stack overflow), inline markup 8 deep (a chain like `_a _a _a ___`, whose closers are valid only at the end of what they enclose, nested once per opener); past that the text stays text. `parse.test.ts` parses 50 KB and 400 KB of each shape that was slow, bounded at 500 ms (a linear scan needs well under 50 ms for 400 KB).
- **Figures** (`Figures.tsx`). A *closed* ```` ```mermaid ```` (or `mmd`) fence is drawn; an open one is the code block with "writing…", so a diagram is rendered once, never per streamed token. A closed ```` ```svg ```` fence is shown as an image. Each figure has Copy (the source), Expand (the lightbox: zoom, pan; the drawing is scaled up to 4× and given the figure's surface colour) and, outside the Android app, Download SVG. A diagram that does not parse stays the code block with a note ("Couldn't draw this diagram: Parse error on line 2"), as does an SVG without an `<svg>` root or over 1.5 M characters.
- **Mermaid** (`diagrams.ts`). `mermaid` is a dynamic import (its diagram types and layout engines are separate chunks fetched when a diagram of that kind first draws: elk's 1.4 MB only for `flowchart-elk`). Rendering is serialised through one queue, `securityLevel: "strict"`, labels as SVG text (`htmlLabels: false`, which also draws correctly once the SVG is an image), `theme: "base"` with `themeVariables` read from the live theme tokens (`mermaidTheme`: bg-deep, tile-3/4, ink, mute, accent, line-3), so Daylight, High contrast and custom themes follow; a theme change redraws (the result cache is keyed by source and `useThemeVersion`, 80 entries, failures included).
- **SVG safety** (`svg.ts`). SVG text, mermaid's output included, is only ever displayed as `<img src="data:image/svg+xml;charset=utf-8,…">`: an SVG loaded as an image runs no script, loads no resource and cannot reach the page's DOM or styles, so there is nothing to sanitise; `prepareSvg` only adds `xmlns`, an explicit size and a `viewBox`. The Tauri CSP already allows `data:` images; no CSP change.
- **Math** (`diagrams.ts`, `Figures.tsx`). `katex` is a dynamic import together with its stylesheet (fonts: woff2 only, `katexWoff2Only` in `vite.config.ts` drops the woff and ttf sources, saving ~1 MB per package). `renderToString` with `trust: false` (no `\href`, `\url`, `\includegraphics`), `maxSize` 30 em, `maxExpand` 1000, `throwOnError: true` (a formula KaTeX rejects shows its TeX and the reason). Until the chunk has loaded, and when it fails to, inline math shows its TeX as code. Results are cached by formula (300).
- **Tool cards** (`tools/preview.ts`, `FilePreview.tsx`, `DiffBody.tsx`). A written or edited `.svg`, `.mmd`/`.mermaid`, or Markdown file that has a diagram, SVG or display-math fence gets Diff / Preview tabs on its file row. The whole new file comes from the call when its patch is the single creating hunk (`newFileContent`): a written SVG or Mermaid file opens as the preview, Markdown opens as the diff. For an edit, which carries only a patch, Preview reads the file from the session's host with `fs.read` (1 MB) and says it is the file as it is now; a relative path resolves against the session's folder.
- **Rendering budget (§6.3).** A message without these blocks costs the scan for `$`/`\(` per inline run and nothing else: no library is loaded and no component mounts. The main and session chunks carry none of mermaid or KaTeX (checked in the production build: both strings absent from them); the first diagram fetches `mermaid.core` (85 kB) plus the chunk of its diagram type (sequence 118 kB, flowchart ~240 kB with its layout shared; gzip a third), the first formula `katex` (259 kB JS, 27 kB CSS, 19 woff2 fonts fetched as used). Rows keep their height across the async draw: a placeholder of 112 px holds the place, the virtualiser's `measureElement` re-measures the row when the figure lands (the same path as images loading), and `estimateRowHeight` counts a mermaid/svg fence as a figure (300 px, 240 on phones) instead of as its lines of source.

### 6.12 What's new after an update (`src/features/updates/{releaseNotes.ts,WhatsNew.tsx,typing.ts}`; Settings → Updates, command palette)
On the first start of a newer version a modal "What's new in TeleFlock x.y.z" shows the GitHub release notes of every release since the last version this device showed (0.7.0 → 0.9.0: 0.9.0, 0.8.1, 0.8.0, newest first, each under its own heading), rendered with the transcript's `Markdown`.
- **Record.** `tf.whatsNew` in localStorage (zustand `persist`, like `tf.prefs`): `seen` (last version whose notes were shown) and `failedStarts`. The check (`useWhatsNewCheck`) runs once the app core has loaded its host profiles. `planStart(seen, current, existing)`, where `existing` = at least one host profile that is not `kind: "demo"` (what every install from before this record, 0.8.x and earlier, has on desktop, Android and the browser/devserver UI, and a fresh install or the demo host hasn't): no record and `!existing` (fresh install, demo host, or an unreadable value on a device without hosts) → record the running version, show nothing, no request; no record and `existing` (updated from an app that never wrote a record) → taken as updated from the previous release: only the running version's notes are due (`from: null`), and the record stays empty until they are dismissed; same or older version than `seen` → nothing (a downgrade keeps the higher mark); newer → fetch. A device that never configured a host before updating counts as fresh. The version is recorded when the modal is dismissed (Got it, Escape, ×; Read on GitHub alone does not count), so quitting with it unseen shows it again at the next start. A demo host in a browser has no real host, so it records silently and shows nothing unless the build's version changed since its record.
- **Source.** The frontend fetches `GET /repos/studioplascode/TeleFlock/releases?per_page=30` anonymously (the updater's endpoint; `connect-src` allows it in both CSPs), at most once per launch (one in-flight request, the answer cached for 30 min for reopening). `releasesBetween` keeps versions above `seen` up to and including the running one (semver precedence incl. pre-releases, `compareVersions`); drafts and tags that aren't versions are dropped; pre-releases only count when the running version is one. A release without notes says so under its heading; none published → the version is recorded silently.
- **Failure.** Offline, a timeout (10 s), a rate limit or a bad answer never blocks start-up (the request runs in the background): the launch is counted in `failedStarts` and the notes stay pending for the next start. At the third failed start a short modal with a link to the release page replaces the notes. Dismissing records the version and clears the count.
- **When.** The modal is mounted with the global overlays (so never over onboarding) and waits (1 s poll) until no dialog, sheet, palette or Answer-next HUD is open (`useUi` plus any open `[role=dialog]`) and the user has not pressed a key in a text field (composer, terminal) for 3 s (`typing.ts`, `canShowNow`).
- **Reopen.** Settings → Updates → What's new (also in the browser, where the section has just this row) and the palette's "What's new" open the running version's notes by hand (cache or one request; the release page link if unavailable); closing records nothing.
- **Layout.** Centred dialog (720 px) with a scrollable body, "Read on GitHub" (`openLink`, system browser in the apps) and "Got it"; a full-screen sheet below the mobile breakpoint.

### 6.13 TeleFlock Browser (app UI, `src/features/browser`; `browser.*`, `AppEvent::Browser`)
The device's browser (core side: `tf_proto` `Browser*`, `BrowserStatus` in the store as `browser`, read with `useBrowser()`; `browser.status` at bootstrap, every `AppEvent::Browser` replaces it; null on a core without it) is shown by the UI itself over CDP: the core gives the browser target's WebSocket (`BrowserStatus.wsUrl`, open to the app's origins only; the Tauri CSPs allow `ws://127.0.0.1:*` in `connect-src`).
- **Controller** (`controller.ts`, one per window, `browserController()`): one `CdpConnection` (`cdp/connection.ts`: request/answer by id with a 15 s timeout, events by name with the flat `sessionId`, pending commands of a detached session rejected) while a surface (split, `#/browser`, fullscreen) is mounted or an observer is registered, 20 s after the last one goes; reconnects 1/2/5/10 s. `Target.setDiscoverTargets` keeps the tab list (`tabs.ts`, pure: popups open after their opener, the shown tab's successor is its opener, closed tabs reopen); every page target gets its own session (`Target.attachToTarget {flatten}`, never auto-attach and never `waitForDebuggerOnStart`) with `Page.enable` and, headless, `Page.setInterceptFileChooserDialog`. A user tab opens in the user's window (a user tab is activated first, else `newWindow`), so it never hides an agent's tab (headless: a tab is `hidden` and casts nothing unless it is its window's active tab; agents' tabs live in their own windows).
- **Screencast.** Only the shown tab casts (`Page.startScreencast` jpeg, quality 80, max size = panel × devicePixelRatio ≤ 2, at most 2560×1600 px per frame); a frame is acknowledged after the viewport drew it (decode with `createImageBitmap`, draw at the next animation frame, a newer frame replaces an undrawn one), so frames never queue. Hidden window: the cast stops; inactive window: every 3rd frame at quality 70 (an agent driving stays visible on a second monitor). The user's own pages lay out for the panel (`Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride`: panel CSS size ÷ zoom, scale = DPR × zoom; zoom per tab, Chrome's steps); an agent's tabs keep the agent's viewport and are letterboxed. Headed mode ("Open in its own window") mirrors the window: no emulation, Chrome's own file dialog. When the shown tab's main frame navigates or another tab becomes the shown one, a frame must follow within 700 ms, else the panel covers the old picture with "Loading the page…" and restarts the cast (`Page.stopScreencast`, then `Page.startScreencast`, which makes Chrome send a fresh frame); a tab the user doesn't drive (an agent's) gets one more restart 700 ms after its first frame (`fresh.ts`). Titles: Chrome 156 sends a page's title only at commit (the URL as placeholder), never when the page sets it later, so while the window is visible and a surface or observer wants frames the controller reads `Target.getTargets` every 1.5 s and applies the titles to the tabs it knows.
- **Input** (`input.ts`, pure; `Viewport.tsx`). Pointer events map through the drawn frame's rectangle into page CSS px (`toPagePoint`, the frame's `deviceWidth/Height`), moves and wheel coalesced per animation frame, click counts as Chrome counts them, pointer capture for drags. Keys go through a hidden textarea at the last click (IME candidates appear there): `routeKey` sends Chrome's own shortcuts to the panel (address bar, tabs, reload, history, zoom, fullscreen), Ctrl+1…8 / Ctrl+J / Ctrl+\ to the app, the rest to the page (`dispatchKeyEvent` with text for typing keys, AltGr, macOS editing commands); composition → `Input.imeSetComposition` then `insertText`; paste is the webview's paste event → `insertText`; copy/cut also copy the page's selection to the device's clipboard. Only the user's own tabs, or an agent's tab they took over, take input; an agent's tab is watched (Take over). While an observer wants element info (a recording), a tab's input goes out in order behind lookups (`controller.ts` `input`): a press, key or text waits until the tab has handled the input before it, then for the element under the pointer or the focused one (`elementScript`), then is sent with its event; a lookup sent alongside input runs before or after it as the page's scheduler likes (measured on Chrome 156: 2 of 200 lookups sent right after Tab saw the old focus, 59 of 200 sent right before a key saw what the key did), so only this order names the field a key or paste went into, also right after a click, Tab or the page moving the focus. Without such an observer input is sent at once.
- **Ownership** (`owners.ts`): tabs missing from `BrowserStatus.tabs` are the user's; the strip groups by owner (You, then each agent in its colour with name, host and a live dot while `activeAt` is under 5 s old). Group menu: Take over / Hand back, Take back lent tabs, Detach, Close its tabs (over CDP); the toolbar: Take over / Hand back on the shown agent tab, "Let an agent use this tab" (`browser.lendTab`) on a user tab. Above the page: each attachment ("<agent> on <host> can control this browser", Driving now, Watch, Detach; collapsed with more than two), requests (Allow for this session / Allow once / Deny), requests waiting at the cap ("Waiting for the browser: N agents use it", Allow anyway = `overCap`), and beside a session "Let <agent> use my browser" (`browser.attach`; `browser_busy` asks to attach anyway). Dialogs of the user's pages are answered in the panel; an agent's page dialog is shown read-only (its agent answers it). File choosers of the user's pages open the app's picker; each file is staged by the core (`browser.stageFile`, ≤ 32 MiB) and set with `DOM.setFileInputFiles`. Downloads show progress, owner, Show in folder (`browser.revealDownload`), Cancel.
- **Requests elsewhere.** An agent's request is a Needs-you `confirm` need (`needs.browser`, options `session`/`turn`/`deny`), so the board, the inbox, Answer next and notification actions answer it with `agent.answer`; the session shows `SessionBrowserAsk` (no dialog exists for it). A device without the browser (Android, a core without it) offers Deny only, on the session card and in every needs-you answer (`needsOptions(needs, hostsBrowser)`); an app core without the browser also strips the Allow actions from the request's notification before it reaches the OS or the UI (`browser::notification_for_device`, called by `on_notify`), leaving Deny and opening the agent.
- **Placement.** Split kind `browser` (session header Browser button, `SplitHeaderRow`), `#/browser` (sidebar, phone More, palette; hidden on Android; opening it closes a browser split, which would show the same browser twice), fullscreen (a portal over the window plus Tauri `setFullscreen` / the Fullscreen API). The tab strip is one flex row: every tab shrinks like Chrome's before it scrolls (an inactive tab down to its icon, 40 px, where its title and close button give way to a container query; the shown tab to 112 px), the new-tab button stays at its right edge (`sticky`), and the shown tab scrolls into view clear of it (`scroll-padding`).
- **Agent menus** (`agentMenu.ts`): the session header's actions and the sidebar's agent menu offer "Let <agent> use my browser" (`browser.attach`; at the cap the core's `browser_busy` asks whether to attach anyway) or "Stop letting <agent> use my browser" (`browser.detach`) for omp agents, on a computer whose core has a browser that is not `unavailable`.
- **Settings → Browser** (`BrowserSettings.tsx`, desktop): the state (Off / Starting / Running, in its own window / Stopped with the core's message) with the engine's name and version and Start / Stop; the engine (`browser.config` engines, Automatic = the first found, or another program's path; `browser.configSet` always sends `enginePath`, `headed` and `cap` together, as it replaces the saved config; Restart now when the running engine differs); the Chrome for Testing download (`browser.installEngine`, progress and failure from `BrowserStatus.install`); the profile and downloads folders (`browser.revealProfile`); "Open in its own window" (`headed`); agents at once (`cap`, 1–16); clearing cookies, the cache or everything (`browser.clearData`, cookies and everything after a confirm that names the lost logins).
- **Observers** (`observe.ts`): `observeBrowser` (user input with element info, in the order of the input; navigations, tabs with owner, console/exception/failed requests of the user's tabs while asked, frames with the page's scroll offset), `captureTab`, `measureElements` (where elements of earlier lookups are now: `measureScript` by ref, the page's registry in the panel's isolated world, which finds one the page replaced again by its selector), `registerBrowserToolbarItem` (session recording). Element info (`cdp/scripts.ts` `elementScript`) is found through open shadow roots and same-origin frames (the selector names each host: `#pay >>> #card`); a frame of another origin is the element itself, `sensitive` as a whole; its rectangle is in the tab's viewport (where the screencast shows it, cut to the frames it is in) with the scroll offset then.
- **Demo.** `?demo` answers `browser.*` in `src/core/demo/browser.ts` (app core) and `browserAgents.ts` (forge's tf-hostd; studio-pc plays one without the browser): running on the in-process fake Chromium (`wsUrl: "demo:browser"`, `demo/fakeBrowser.ts`, canned pages painted into JPEG frames, scripted agent steps; a navigation is a Document request for sessions that watch the network: 200, the 404 of a missing page, Chrome's `net::ERR_NAME_NOT_RESOLVED` / `net::ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED` for an address nothing answers, the failures also as the console's "Failed to load resource"; the shop's Upload without a file fails with a 500, a console error and an exception, so a recording has errors to report), downloads in `/home/demo/Downloads`, the address bar searching `https://search.teleflock.demo/?q=`. quota and ci are attached and drive their tabs in bursts while they work; expenses asks (a needs-you with `browser`, a `use_user_browser` tool row). Allow attaches it (an `agent.answer` of the request is routed to `browser.answer`, as tf-client does) and it opens its own tab in its own window (`openDemoAgentTab`); Deny ends its tool call with the bridge's words; the cap queues; an Allow once attachment ends with the agent's turn; detach, take over, hand back and lending change the reported owners. Engines, the download, clearing and the config are simulated.

### 6.14 Chat tasks in the app (`src/core/chats.ts`, `src/features/chats`, Dispatch; host side §3.31)
A chat is an omp agent with no repository or folder of the user's (`Agent.chat`). `src/core/chats.ts` holds what every surface shares: `CHAT_REPO` (`"chat:"`, Dispatch's "No folder · chat" choice where a repository root or id goes in thread keys, draft keys and `lastRepo`), `CHAT_TARGET` (`{kind: "chat"}`), `isChat`, `hostTakesChats` (`HostInfo.chats` present), `CHATS_NEED_UPDATE` and `chatsUnsupported` (an older daemon's `invalid_params` "unknown variant `chat`" or `unknown_method` on `chat.*`).
- **Starting.** Dispatch's repo chip offers "No folder · chat" for a host and for an Auto group, disabled with the update words when no member starts chats; "New chat" in the palette and in the phone's create sheet (`dispatch/openChat.ts`) picks it for Dispatch's current target and focuses the task box. A chat thread's send is one `agent.start {target: {kind: "chat"}, spec}` with the spec a dispatch's agent gets (model, thinking, plan, template framing, own model roles and fallback chains, decide for me, images, videos), named as dispatches are (omp's tiny model, else the prompt's slug, deduped against the host's agents and dispatches; the host keeps the name, numbering it only when an agent took it meanwhile). Auto picks among the group's online hosts that start chats with the usual workload and quota terms. No branch, base, trust or worktree; a retry links the chat's own agent when it is on the board, else starts it again. Quick capture sends to a chat thread the same way.
- **Showing.** Board cards, phone rows, needs cards, Wall tiles, the session header and the phone session's subtitle show a "Chat" chip where repo · branch go; no diff meter, no Review, no worktree removal, no Move; the inspector lists changed files without Review links; ReviewView on a chat's workspace makes no git call. Repo grouping puts every chat in one Chats column. Mark done, Undo, resume, rename and the terminal work as for any agent.
- **Deleting.** "Delete chat…" (card menu, phone row and session menus, the Cleanup page's Chats section from `chat.list`) runs `chat.delete` as a dry run, confirms what goes and what stays (the pane, the card, the folder or what keeps it, another card in the folder, the conversation staying in Past sessions), then deletes; the deleted chat arrives in `HostInfo.pruned`, so drafts, notifications and Dispatch entries of it go. A Cleanup row without a card deletes by folder, and the host deletes an agent working there with it: when the dry run says one works there (the chat was resumed after the list was read), nothing is deleted, a toast says so and the list is read again, so the row shows its card and Delete goes through the agent's own confirm.
- **Older hosts and apps.** Without `HostInfo.chats` the choices are disabled with "update the host" words, and a send that still reaches such a host fails with them. An app from before chats shows a chat as an agent in a folder under `~/.teleflock/chats` without a repo.
- **Demo.** forge starts chats (seeded: a chat in Review whose folder keeps a file, one in Done, two folders without a card); studio-pc answers like a daemon from before chats.

### 6.15 Session recording (`recording.*`, `AppEvent::Recordings`; app `src/features/recording`, core `tf-client` `recording/`, demo `src/core/demo/recordings.ts`)
