Record in the browser toolbar walks an agent through a problem: a video of the user's own tab(s), their voice, a timestamped log of what they did and what failed, merged into one timeline that a new Dispatch task (or an existing agent) receives with the files on its host. Wire types `tf_proto::recording`. Nothing leaves the device until the user sends.
- **Capture** (`recorder.ts`). `recording.start {voice?}` makes the folder and starts the microphone (tf-dictation `MicRecorder`, 16 kHz mono PCM16 `voice.wav`, feature `voice` of tf-client; without it, or when the microphone fails, the recording runs without voice and says why). The recorder registers an observer (`observeBrowser`, §6.13: console, network, elements, frames) and feeds `model.ts` `EventLog`; `t` of everything is recording time (ms since `createdAt`, pauses left out, `recordingTime`). Twice a second `recording.append {frames, events, shots}` hands over what is final (`take(at)`: entries stay changeable for 4 s for repeats, double clicks, redirects and titles).
- **Event log** (`model.ts`, stored as opaque JSON lines). One entry per typed run per field (Backspace edits it, Enter ends it as submitted, a gap of 2.5 s or another field starts a new one), per scroll (gaps < 1 s), per click (double clicks stay one; > 8 px between down and up is a drag), keys worth a line (Enter, Escape, arrows, shortcuts; repeats counted), tab switches, `ui` actions (address entered, Back/Reload), navigations (one within 1.5 s with no input between is a redirect of the previous), console errors/warnings and uncaught exceptions with source and stack, failed requests (status ≥ 400 or a network error, with up to 300 characters of the response; `ERR_ABORTED` and favicons left out), dialogs. The same error within 2 s is counted; at most 300 errors are logged, the rest counted.
- **Privacy.** Typing into password, card and one-time-code fields (the browser's `sensitive`, `type=password`, `autocomplete` `current-password`/`new-password`/`one-time-code`/`cc-*`, names/labels like password, PIN, CVC, IBAN, API key, or a frame of another origin, which the panel can't look into: hosted card fields, 3-D Secure) is logged as hidden with no characters, and their keys never make a line, shortcuts included (Ctrl+Alt is AltGr on Windows). A key with text is typing unless Ctrl or Meta is held (Option types characters on a Mac). Each key and paste names the field it went into, found through open shadow roots and same-origin frames and looked up after the tab handled the input before it (§6.13 Input): typing right after a click, Tab or a page moving the focus is the new field's. A label that is a sensitive element's own text is left out. Typed or pasted text that looks like a credential (JWTs, provider keys, GitHub/Slack tokens, AWS key ids, Bearer values, long mixed-case-digit runs) is hidden too and scrubbed from error texts; URLs lose user/password, the values of credential-like parameters (`***`, also in an OAuth fragment), and path segments, fragment segments and bare queries that are credentials by the same rules (magic, reset and webhook links; a lowercase hex token is not one). The video covers the focused sensitive field and every field sensitive input went into on the page (a hidden secret pasted or typed into an ordinary field too, and input while paused), also once the focus moved on: before each picture they are measured again (`measureElements`, at most 300 ms, else where they were last) and covered where the picture shows them; one measured at another scroll offset than the picture's is covered both where it was and where that scroll moved it (it may sit in a fixed box). A field inside a closed shadow root is judged by its host. Agents' tabs are never recorded: switching to one logs "watching <agent>'s tab" and the video shows a card instead; input there is dropped.
- **Video** (`compose.ts`, core `recording/mov.rs`). Screencast frames of the shown user tab are drawn at most 10 times a second into one fixed picture (the first frame's size, ≤ 1280 px wide, letterboxed when the panel changes) under a strip with the page's address and title, with a ring for 0.8 s where the user clicked and the covers (Privacy, cut to the page's area); JPEG 0.72. At stop the core writes `recording.mov`: QuickTime, Photo-JPEG at a constant 10 fps where an unchanged slot points at the previous frame's bytes, plus the voice as 16 kHz PCM. Measured: ffmpeg (omp's `read` on the host: `-ss T -i f -frames:v 1`) returns the first frame at or after T, so variable frame rate (AVI zero-length chunks, Matroska timestamps) showed the next change or nothing; the shared-offset MOV gives the frame visible at T, and every frame is a keyframe, so trims and cuts are frame-exact without re-encoding. MediaRecorder in the webview was not used: no audio from the native microphone, and cuts would need re-encoding.
- **Screenshots**: at the first picture, at each click (the page as clicked with the covers that picture was drawn with, ring and element outlined), 0.8 s after a page loads, 0.4 s after an error (at most every 4 s), at stop; ≤ 60 per recording, JPEG 0.85, logged as `shot` entries.
- **Stop and transcription.** `recording.stop` → `processing` (mux) → `ready`; with voice the transcript runs on the device with the user's dictation model (tf-dictation `transcribe`, VAD segments, `voice.wav` read half a second at a time: an hour of voice is never in memory whole) → `transcript.segments` in recording time (`voice.offsetMs` added). No model: `noModel` (Settings → Dictation; `recording.transcribe` runs it again). A transcription the app ended in (quit, or crashed: out of memory, say) runs again at the next start; `meta.json` counts the runs that never ended (`transcribeRuns`, written before each run, cleared when one ends), and after two the next start marks it `failed` ("The app ended twice while transcribing this recording") instead of running it at every start; `recording.transcribe` runs it again. The core emits `AppEvent::Recordings` on every state change and transcription progress (≤ 2/s).
- **Timeline** (`timeline.ts`). Log and transcript merged in time order (speech first at the same moment), minus the review's removed parts (trim + cuts: what happened or was said in them goes, later times move up, `mapTime`) and hidden lines. `timeline.md` (header: when, length, start page, the video and how to seek it, `- \`00:14.0\` clicked button "Save" (button#save) at 640,412`, `said: "…"`, `POST /api/x → 500 …`, screenshot paths; then Errors and failed requests, and the transcript) and `timeline.json` (`teleflock-recording/1`: entries with their fields, transcript, shots, removed ranges). Times are `mm:ss.d`, which omp's video selectors read (`clip.mov:00:14.0`).
- **Review** (`ReviewSheet.tsx`, opens after Stop; Recordings in the toolbar menu and Settings → Recordings). Player (frames from `recording.frame`, a few ahead while playing; voice from `voice.wav` in step; removed parts skipped), scrubber with errors, clicks, pages and speech, Start here / End here, Remove a part (mark, then the end), hide/show per timeline line, the task text (what was said; Rambler's rewrite when Settings → Dictation → Rambler is on or the switch is turned on, `suggestRamble` with the first online host; edits kept), Delete, Save for later, Send to agent…, New task in Dispatch. Edits are saved in `RecordingInfo.doc` (`recording.update`). Sending waits for the transcript unless the user chooses not to.
- **Sending** (`send.ts`). The recording attaches to the composer as a draft video with a source (`attachVideoSource`, §3.13 / `draftVideos.ts`): chip "uploads when sent"; up to 4 key screenshots go as images (errors first, then clicks just before an error, start, end, pages). Dispatch: `openPrefilledTask` with source `recording` (banner, Send to agent… moves it); an agent: its composer, focused. Only when the prompt goes out (Dispatch Auto: on the host picked) does it upload: `recording.render {cuts, files}` into a folder of that upload's own, `bundle/<render>` (`RecordingBundle.dir`: a cut `recording.mov` there when parts were removed, and the render's `timeline.md`/`.json`), so sending the recording to another host meanwhile renders again beside it and never writes over a file an upload reads; then the video (`UploadSource` reading `recording.read` in 1 MiB chunks; a read whose file size differs from the render's fails the upload rather than sending parts of two files), ≤ 24 screenshots, and the timeline written with their paths on that host; the prompt gets the task text and the block `recordingRef` (timeline path first, the JSON twin, the video with its seek hint, the screenshots, why there is no transcript, and the timeline inline up to 60 lines / 6000 characters). A failed or aborted upload drops what went up. When the upload ends, however it ends, `recording.release {id, dir}` removes the render's folder; the next start of the app removes renders left behind. A recording in a draft comes back after a restart (`registerVideoSource("recording")`, imported in `main.tsx`).
- **Storage** (`<data_dir>/recordings/<id>/`: `meta.json`, `events.jsonl`, `frames.idx` + frames (in `frames.bin` while recording, in `recording.mov` after), `voice.wav`, `shots/`, `bundle/<render>/`). The recordings folder and everything in it are this user's only: 0700 folders and 0600 files on Unix (`util::private_dir`, `private_options`, `write_private`; `voice.wav` too, as tf-dictation writes it), like the session cache (§8); a recordings folder made before is tightened at the next Record. One recording at a time; 1 GiB or 60 min per recording and 2 GiB for all (`recording.start` refuses with `budget_exceeded`). The app core checks the recording's limits every second while it records, whether or not appends come (a still page sends none while the voice keeps growing, about 115 MB an hour), and at one stops the recording itself as `recording.stop` would (microphone and files closed, `processing`, `AppEvent::Recordings`); `recording.append` answers `stop` with the reason, also after that stop, and `recording.stop` then answers the stopped recording. The recorder asks with an append when the list shows its recording stopped, then shows the reason and finishes as for any limit (the review opens). Nothing is deleted by itself; Delete in the lists. A recording left running by a crash or quit is finished at the next start.
- **Demo.** `?demo` keeps recordings in memory: one is there from the start ("Checkout total stays at 0", drawn on a canvas, with a transcript, a 500 and an exception), and a live one gets a canned transcript after a short processing phase.

### 6.16 On-device dictation (`dictation.*`, `AppEvent::Dictation`, `AppEvent::DictationSession`; crate `tf-dictation`, core `tf-client` `dictation/`, app `src/features/dictation`, demo `src/core/demo/dictation.ts`)
The composers' mic turns speech into text on the device itself, offline, with a speech model the user picks per device in Settings → Dictation; what is said never leaves the device (the only network use is the model download). The desktop apps (Windows, macOS, Linux: tf-client feature `voice`, §5.2) and devservers built with `--features voice` (§5.3) carry the engine. The Android app keeps its system speech recognizer (§5.4; the engine's Android libraries would add about 26.5 MB to the APK), browsers the Web Speech API. Rambler (§6.10) works on top of every source. Wire types `tf_proto::dictation`; native dependencies and builds: docs/build.md §10.5.
- **Engine** (`tf-dictation`: `engine.rs`, `sys.rs`). sherpa-onnx 2.0.1 over onnxruntime, through its C API: offline recognizers (NeMo transducer, NeMo Canary, Whisper, Moonshine v2; int8 exports, CPU, greedy search) and the Silero voice activity detector, embedded in the binary. One recognizer is loaded at a time, keyed by model and language and shared by sessions and transcriptions (`lib.rs` `OneLoaded`): a load in progress makes every other caller wait for it, so callers of the model share that one load and another model loads after it; it is freed after 5 minutes unused, before its model is deleted (after a load in progress), and before another model loads. A session or transcription still using the old one keeps it until it ends, so two can be in memory meanwhile (a dictation with another model or language while a recording is transcribed: Canary on Detect automatically hears the device locale's language when dictating and English when transcribing); waiting for it instead would hold a dictation for as long as a transcription runs. The Silero model is written to `<data_dir>/dictation/silero_vad.onnx` once (first uses at the same time wait for the writer; a file of the right size is it). A decode uses half the cores, 1 to 4 threads. Text is trimmed; a model's silence markers and lone punctuation are dropped. Chosen over whisper.cpp on the measurements below: Whisper's most accurate model costs seconds per utterance on a CPU, while Parakeet and Canary return a sentence in well under a second, and sherpa-onnx runs all of them from prebuilt static libraries for every desktop target.
- **Models** (`catalog.rs`). Hugging Face exports, every file pinned by repository commit, size and sha256 (`huggingface.co/<repo>/resolve/<commit>/<file>`):

  | Model (`id`) | Languages | Download | Memory | Notes |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | Parakeet TDT 0.6B v3 (`parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3`) | 25 European, detected | 670 MB | ~1.1 GB | recommended on desktops |
  | Canary 180M Flash (`canary-180m-flash`) | English, German, French, Spanish; told which | 207 MB | ~450 MB | recommended on phones (no phone core has the engine) |
  | Whisper large-v3 turbo (`whisper-turbo`) | 99, detected | 1.04 GB | ~1.8 GB | most accurate; "Slow for live dictation" (`speed` ≤ 2) |
  | Whisper small (`whisper-small`) | 99, detected | 375 MB | ~700 MB | "Slow for live dictation" (`speed` ≤ 2: 4–5 s a sentence) |
  | Whisper base (`whisper-base`) | 99, detected | 161 MB | ~350 MB | |
  | Moonshine base, English (`moonshine-base-en`) | English | 141 MB | ~300 MB | fastest |

  `speed` and `accuracy` (1–5) and each model's `notes` come from these measurements (the engine spike and this check, 2026-10/11: a Ryzen 7 5800X, 4 threads, while other builds kept the load at 8–30, so speeds are pessimistic). Word error rate (lowercased, punctuation removed) on FLEURS read speech (20 clips per language) and on synthetic dictation spoken by Piper voices (4 developer prompts per language, 6 German ones full of English terms); RTF is decode time ÷ audio length:

  | Model, engine | FLEURS en | FLEURS de | Dictation en | Dictation de | German + English terms | RTF |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | Parakeet TDT 0.6B v3, sherpa-onnx | 4.5 % | 15.0 % (2 of 20 clips, which start with English names, came out in English) | 2.2 % | 1.4 % | 14.2 % | 0.05–0.18 |
  | Canary 180M Flash, sherpa-onnx | 5.2 % | 7.4 % | 0 % | 4.3 % | 23.9 % | 0.08–0.14 |
  | Whisper large-v3 turbo, whisper.cpp q5_0 | 3.9 % | 4.1 % | 4.4 % | 0 % | 8.0 % | 2.6–5.4 (a full 30 s window per utterance: ~30 s per clip) |
  | Whisper small, sherpa-onnx | 5.0 % | 10.2 % | 2.2 % | 5.8 % | 23.0 % | 0.38–0.83 |
  | Whisper base, sherpa-onnx | 6.5 % | 27.2 % | 4.4 % | 13.0 % | 29.2 % | 0.10–0.27 |
  | Moonshine base (English), sherpa-onnx | 7.9 % | — | 4.4 % | — | — | 0.03–0.05 |
  | Qwen3-ASR 0.6B, sherpa-onnx (not offered) | 4.2 % | 9.2 % | 2.2 % | 4.3 % | 25.7 % | 0.22–0.35 |
  | Omnilingual ASR 300M CTC, sherpa-onnx (not offered) | 12.3 % | 20.6 % | 14.4 % | 8.7 % | 35.4 % | 0.08–0.14 |
- **Language** (`Model::language_for`). Settings offers Detect automatically and 18 common languages some model knows (`DictationState.languages`). Whisper keeps to a chosen language it knows and detects otherwise; Canary needs one: the chosen one (English when it doesn't know it), on Detect automatically the device locale's (`dictation.start {locale}`, the page's `navigator.language`) when it knows that, else English. Parakeet and Moonshine ignore the choice (`takesLanguage` false).
- **Downloads and storage** (`store.rs`; `<data_dir>/dictation/`). `models/<id>/<file>`, downloading as `<file>.part` and resumed with an HTTP range request (a server that ignores the range starts it over); every file must match its size and sha256, else it is deleted and the reason kept for Settings (Retry); `.complete` holds the commit, written after the last file passed, and a model counts as installed only with the catalog's current commit there and every file at its size. Cancel keeps the `.part` files (Settings offers Resume). At start the app core removes what the catalog no longer owns: dropped models, files a model no longer lists, models pinned to an older commit. `dictation.json` (written atomically) holds the chosen model, the language and the microphone. HTTP is rustls on ring with the Mozilla roots, as the updater; debug builds may download from `TF_DICTATION_MIRROR` (labs).
- **Microphone** (`mic.rs`, `audio.rs`). One capture per process, shared by dictation and recordings (§6.15): cpal on ALSA, CoreAudio or WASAPI, opened with the first consumer and closed after the last; downmixed and resampled to 16 kHz mono (a streaming windowed-sinc resampler as Kaldi's, under 1 ms of delay). The device is the one chosen in Settings (`dictation.setMicrophone`, saved with its name) while it is connected, else the system default; opening waits up to 10 s (a system permission prompt) and fails with `permission` or `no_device`. Overruns and a changed default device don't stop a capture; any other device error ends it for every consumer, with the reason. Debug builds play WAV files instead (`TF_DICTATION_FAKE_MIC`).
- **Sessions** (`session.rs`, `segment.rs`). `Session::start` checks the chosen model and opens the microphone (an error here: nothing started, no event), reports `listening`, then loads the detector and the recognizer on a thread of its own and listens. The detector (Silero, threshold 0.5) is stepped one 32 ms window at a time whatever the batch (`segment::WINDOW`; sherpa-onnx is fed per window too), so audio that piled up while a model loaded or a decode ran is placed where it was said: the first words survive, and the first partial after the pile already holds everything said so far. It ends an utterance at a 0.5 s pause (shorter than 0.25 s is no speech; longer than 20 s is split), and each utterance takes along the 0.3 s of audio before its detected start (never reaching into the previous one), since Silero hears soft vowel onsets ("Öffne") about 0.2 s late; each finished utterance is decoded once: `final`. While one is spoken, every further 0.5 s asks for a decode of it so far (from 0.5 s before the detector noticed the speech): `partial`, skipped when the measured decode speed says it would take more than 350 ms, so a final never waits behind it. A session ends after `stop` (what is being said is still decoded, and so is all audio still waiting: a stop while the model loads, seconds for a large one cold, keeps everything said meanwhile), `cancel` (dropped; the microphone closes at once, also while the models load, and a cancel during the detector's load skips the recognizer's), a minute without speech, the microphone going away (an `error`; what was said is kept) or an engine error; `end` is always last. Recordings are transcribed with the same detector (`transcribe`, §6.15), so their segments also start up to 0.3 s before the detected speech.
- **App core** (`tf-client` `dictation/`). `dictation.models` → `DictationState`: the catalog with each model's `phase` (`available`, `downloading`, `installed`), `downloaded` (bytes of a download in progress or interrupted), the last download `error` and `recommended` for this device class; `selected` (an installed model only; absent: none, the mic uses the system's recognition), `language`, `languages`, `engine` (`sherpa-onnx 2.0.1`), `microphone`. `dictation.install {modelId}` starts or resumes a download on a thread of its own and answers at once; the first model installed becomes the chosen one when no installed model is. `dictation.cancelInstall`. `dictation.remove` (refused with `busy` while downloading) of the chosen model cancels its sessions and waits up to 3 s for them to let go of the files (Windows can't delete open files), then hands over to another installed model, the one recommended for the device first, else none. `dictation.select {modelId?}` (installed only), `dictation.setLanguage`, `dictation.microphones` (the system default first), `dictation.setMicrophone {id?}` (a connected one; applies from the next capture). `dictation.start {session, locale?}` (the page's id, 1–64 characters, not running), `dictation.stop`, `dictation.cancel` (an unknown id is a no-op). Start errors: `no_model` (hint "Download a speech model in Settings → Dictation."), `permission` (hint: where to allow the microphone on macOS and Windows), `no_device`, `unavailable` while shutting down. One session at a time: a start stops the running one. A session is registered before its microphone opens (which can wait for the system's permission prompt), so a stop, a cancel, a page close or another start meanwhile reaches it once it started instead of leaving the microphone open.
- **Choosing the source** (`useDictation`, `engineInUse`). A text box's mic uses the app core's engine while a model is installed and chosen (`chosenModel`), else the platform's (Android's recognizer, Web Speech); a session keeps its engine until it ends, whatever changes in Settings meanwhile. The engine's mic shows wherever the app core has a chosen model.
- **Sessions in the page** (`DeviceSession`, `store.ts`). The page picks a random id (`crypto.randomUUID`; tabs sharing a devserver never collide) and routes `AppEvent::DictationSession` events by it from before `dictation.start` is sent; a stop or cancel while the start is on its way is sent once it answered. Partials replace the interim text, each final goes to `onFinal`, `end` to `onEnd`. A failed start shows its message and hint; after `no_model` the error offers **Open settings** (Settings opens with the Dictation card brought into view). `dictation.models` is read once and every `AppEvent::Dictation` replaces it; `unsupported` / `unknown_method` mark the core as without models.
- **Settings → Dictation** (`DictationSettings.tsx`, `models.ts`). Speech recognition: System or On this device (switching picks the recommended installed model, else the first). One row per model: size, languages, memory, the speed and accuracy meters, summary and notes, In use / Recommended / Slow for live dictation; Download (primary for the recommended model while none is chosen), the progress bar with Cancel, Resume of an interrupted download, Retry with the failure, Use, Delete behind a confirm that names the model taking over. Language: for a model that takes one, only the languages it knows (a choice made for another model stays listed, marked "doesn't know it"), and a note saying what the chosen model hears with the current choice (`heardLanguage` mirrors `Model::language_for`: "Canary 180M Flash doesn't know Italian: it listens for English."). Microphone: System default (with its device's name) and the devices listed when the card opens and on refresh; a chosen one that isn't connected stays listed, "not connected: the default listens". Then Rambler's rows. A core without models gets one row naming what listens instead (Android's recognizer; the browser's Web Speech, with the note that Chrome and Edge send the voice to Google or Microsoft; nothing) and where speech models are (the desktop app, TeleFlock 0.9.0).
- **Demo** (`src/core/demo/dictation.ts`, `?demo`). The real catalog; downloads of a few seconds with progress, cancel and resume; sessions that hear a canned English or German ramble (a word partial every 260 ms, a final 300 ms after each utterance, Stop finishing the utterance, the idle end after a minute); an Android user agent gets `unsupported`, as the Android app does.
- **Tests.** `cargo test -p tf-dictation`: catalog pins, downloads against a local server (resume, a server ignoring ranges, a corrupt file, cancel, progress at most 4 times a second), the sweep, segmentation (a backlog of audio placed per window), the session loop with a fake detector and decoder (order, stop, cancel, a lost microphone, a stop waiting with its audio when listening begins, a cancel during either load), one load at a time shared by its callers, the voice activity model written once by first uses at the same time, the fake microphone shared by consumers, the resampler, WAV files read whole or a piece at a time, the engine linked with its detector on silence and on recorded German sentences (arriving all at once; a soft vowel start, kept by the lead-in). `cargo test -p tf-client --features voice --lib -- dictation:: recording::`: sessions under the page's id, stop and cancel, one at a time, a session ending while it starts, start errors with hints, calls that cross a start, a closed page, choosing and removing models with the handover, the microphone choice, a recording's voice through the shared microphone, limits that stop a recording no append reaches, renders per upload and their release, transcriptions not run again after they ended the app twice. `bun test src/core/demo/dictation.test.ts src/features/dictation src/features/session/composer/useDictation.test.ts`: the demo's engine, `DeviceSession` (calls that cross a start included), the model choices and language rules of Settings, the engine choice. Lab: `scripts/lab/dictation/setup.sh [--ui] [--models DIR] [--mic FILES] [--install IDS] dict…`, then `bun scripts/lab/dictation/drive.ts <lab>`: tf-devserver with `--features voice` and a fake microphone (by default English and German sessions spoken by Piper voices, `target/shared-voice/samples/dictation/session-{en,de}.wav` on the lab box, not in the repository), models from Hugging Face or, with `--models`, from a local mirror of sherpa-onnx's release directories, Rambler through the lab host's mock models.

### 6.17 Agent chat message board (app, `src/features/session/irc`; `session.agentChat`, `SessionMeta.agentChat`; host side §3.34, demo `src/core/demo/agentChat.ts`)
The board shows every omp IRC message of a session's whole agent tree, not only what reached the main agent.
- **Copy of the log** (`store.ts`, `chat.ts`). One per agent key, shared by the inspector card, the phone's Chat tab and the board. The first fetch is `session.agentChat {agentId}` (the whole log); after it, whenever `SessionMeta.agentChat` names a newer `rev` or another `epoch`, `{agentId, epoch, after: rev}` returns what changed after it (`mergeChatPage`: by id, time order, the oldest beyond 2,000 dropped; a `reset` page or a new epoch replaces the copy). One request per session at a time; a change announced meanwhile fetches once more after it. `live: false` (the session's bridge doesn't tap omp's IRC bus, so tf-hostd reads the files at each fetch): again every 3 s while the board shows, every 10 s while the card does, and when the session's own IRC rows change. A failed fetch keeps the copy (or, with none, shows the transcript's rows) with the error and Retry; the next announced revision tries again.
- **Older hosts.** No `SessionMeta.agentChat`: the session's own IRC rows (`fromTranscript`: Main's sends with their tool status as pending / failed, what reached it, replies linked by omp message id), with "Only <agent>'s own messages: this host runs a TeleFlock before 0.9.0".
- **Who is who** (`agentNamer`). Senders and recipients are omp ids: a roster subagent by id, else by name, gets its short name and the subagents it runs under (`groupSubagents`: `parentId`, else the dotted id); `Main` is the session's name; `user` is "You"; an id the roster lacks reads from its dots.
- **Opening** (`boardUi.tsx`). The card (title, Open message board, a preview: at that message), the session menu's "Message board" (the phone's ⋯ sheet too) and the palette's "Open agent message board", the last two for sessions with subagents or agent chat. Desktop: the split panel beside the session (`SplitState` kind `agentChat`, `SplitHeaderRow` with Full screen and the host chip); phones and focus mode: `FullscreenFrame` (`AgentChatOverlay`, once per app). The board keeps the session subscribed while it shows (`openSession`), so a board left beside another view stays live.
- **Board** (`MessageBoard.tsx`, `TranscriptLog` with its own row estimates). Rows as described in the feature README; unread is per session on this device (`tf.ircSeen` v1: the newest `at` the board showed, under host + omp session id once known, so a parked agent resumed under a new agent id keeps it, else under the agent key, whose mark moves to the session's at the next read; v0's per-peer marks carry over as their newest), messages from `user` never count; everything shown counts as read while the window is visible. Filters by one agent (involving it) or two (between them) and text; a reply's quote or a revealed message filtered away clears the filters first.
- **Sending.** `agent.subagent {action: "chat", message}` to the chosen subagent, or to every running one with `broadcast: true` (one request each; hosts ≥ 0.9.0 log one message from `user`, older ones ignore the flag). Partial failures toast which subagents missed it.
- **Demo.** forge's sessions answer `session.agentChat`; pool's tree talks while its task runs (Main and its scouts, PoolBench orchestrating Sweep and Summary, Sweep its three benches, scouts asking each other, PoolBench's broadcast, replies, a progress note, wake relays, Sweep's retry refused because Size64 had ended); other sessions' logs are their transcript rows; studio-pc answers like a host before 0.9.0.
- **Tests.** `bun test src/features/session/irc src/core/demo/agentChat.test.ts`: merging pages (replace, append, reorder, epochs, cap), transcript rows, names and tree positions, participants' order, filters, unread, receipts, read marks with the v0 migration and the cap; the demo log's tree, pages and the user's broadcast.

## 7. Testing and verification
- **Golden fixtures.** Collab frames (`docs/research/omp-collab-samples/`) feed `tf-collab` replica tests. herdr samples (`docs/research/herdr-samples/`) feed `tf-herdr` parser tests.
- **Lab sessions.** E2E runs use the herdr session `tf-lab` (never `default`), with a dedicated hostd instance through `TF_HOSTD_HOME=/tmp/tf-lab-home` (the env var overrides `~/.teleflock`). The relay uses a non-default port in the lab (`--relay-port 47566`) so it never collides with a real daemon.
- **UI verification.** Headless Chromium against the devserver (real host = this machine, lab session) and against the demo host, at 1440×900 and 412×892.

## 8. Security model (post-review, phase D)

**Trust boundary.** Whoever can reach a host's TeleFlock surfaces can drive that host's agents and shells. Every surface is therefore bound to the host user:

| Surface | Protection |
|---|---|
| App ⇄ host | **Tailscale SSH** (default): tailnet identity, no stored credentials. Hosts set to key or password sign-in keep those secrets on the device only (`secrets.json`, 0600, never returned or logged, §5.1), and send them only after the host key is pinned or confirmed. |
| Host keys | Tailnet addresses (100.64.0.0/10, fd7a:115c:a1e0::/48) are pinned on first use silently, because Tailscale authenticates the node. Any other address stops at `ConnPhase::HostKey` until the user confirms the fingerprint (`hosts.trustKey`). A changed key is always an error. |
| `tf-hostd` sockets | Unix sockets are 0600 inside a 0700 `run/` dir. On Windows, both pipes have a DACL for the current user SID plus SYSTEM, and the Rust client refuses a pipe owned by another user. |
| Companion extension | hostd writes `run/bridge.secret` (0600 / user ACL). The extension accepts requests only after the secret handshake (`docs/bridge-protocol.md`). |
| Collab relay (`127.0.0.1:47466`) | Loopback only. Requests carrying an `Origin` header (browsers) get 403. Caps: 256 rooms, 512 connections, 32 MiB queued per guest. hostd checks its own relay with a `/healthz?nonce=` proof before joining rooms. A failed bind is surfaced as a setup issue. Room keys never leave the host. |
| Files | `~/.teleflock` is 0700; `state.json`, logs and the secret are 0600; deploys run under `umask 077`. Uploaded binaries are verified with sha256 on the host before the atomic `mv`. On the device, the session cache (`data_dir/sessions`: transcripts, tool output included) and the recordings (`data_dir/recordings`: video, voice, screenshots, the typed log, transcripts and renders, §6.15) are 0700 folders with 0600 files on Unix, like `secrets.json`. |
| git | Read-only calls run with `core.fsmonitor=false` and hooks off. Diff/show/log add `--no-textconv --no-ext-diff`. **Folder browsing never runs git**: badges come from `.git`/`HEAD` on disk, because filter drivers can't be disabled per call. |
| Preview tunnels | Bound to `127.0.0.1` on the device; targets are restricted to host loopback; tunnels close on disconnect. The Android app shows a tunnel's page in a window of its own, never in a frame of its page (Framed content). |
| `tf-devserver` | Per-launch 256-bit token, Origin and Host checks, loopback-only unless `--allow-remote`. |
| Webview | Strict CSP. Agent-controlled links (markdown, web sources, xterm OSC 8) allow only `http(s)` and open externally through `app.openExternal`; other schemes render inert. `TF_HOSTD_BIN` deploy override works in debug builds only. Android has backups disabled (`allowBackup=false`, data-extraction rules exclude everything); cleartext traffic is allowed only to loopback (the preview window). Presented pages (§3.32) run in sandboxed frames under a policy without network loads; CSP covers neither WebRTC nor resource hints, so the desktop apps remove WebRTC from every sandboxed frame (`frames.rs`), the page's markup loses its resource hints and its scripts can't add them through the DOM, and Linux turns WebKitGTK's preconnects off. A page written to get out can still preconnect (a DNS lookup, a TCP/TLS handshake) or, in Chromium, prerender from markup its scripts parse from strings on Windows, on macOS and in a browser, where a frame it makes can also use WebRTC (§3.32 has the exact list). Save and Open in browser write a page with the policy first and never a file a default app would run (`.txt` appended); `doc_open`'s folder is per account (`teleflock-docs-<uid>` in the temporary folder on Unix). |
| Framed content | Whether a frame of the app's page can call the app's commands depends on how each WebView hands Tauri's IPC to frames (read from the pinned wry 0.57.0 and tauri 2.12.4 sources; no Android device or emulator was available to run it). **Android: every frame can.** wry passes Tauri's init scripts, the IPC script with its invoke key included, to `addDocumentStartJavaScript(…, setOf("*"))`, which runs them in every frame of every origin (Tauri's main-frame-only flag is dropped), and `addJavascriptInterface` puts the `ipc` bridge object into every frame. `Ipc.kt` names the caller by the top page's URL (`currentUrl`, set in the main frame's `onPageStarted`), so Tauri sees `http://tauri.localhost`, a local origin, and runs what the main window may: every app command (`core_call`: any app or host method, prompts and answers included, given the ids; `core_term_input`; `doc_*`, `drafts_*`) and every plugin command of its capabilities (`core:default`, `deep-link:default`, `teleflock-android:default`: dictation, permission prompts, share sheet, taken shares …). Replies are evaluated in the top page, so a framed page acts blind, but it acts. No IPC-side check can tell frames apart there, so on Android nothing fetched is framed: tunnels open in the preview window (`PreviewActivity`, a plain WebView without IPC, §5.4), presented pages run without scripts (`sandbox=""`, §3.32), and `tauri.android.conf.json` narrows the page's `frame-src` to the app's own `blob:` documents (the demo host's previews; `srcdoc` frames are not fetched), checked by `cargo test -p teleflock --lib csp`. **Desktop: no frame can.** WebKitGTK and WKWebView run Tauri's scripts in the top frame only, so a frame has no invoke key, and WKWebView names the caller by the calling frame's URL (remote: refused). WebView2 runs them in every frame, but a frame's call goes out as a fetch carrying its own `Origin` (not local: refused, a `null` origin is no URL) and its `postMessage` reaches no handler (wry listens to the top document's only). A narrow window's preview sheet (an iframe of `http://127.0.0.1:<port>`) and the `tfdoc:` frames therefore stay as they are. |
| Host config | Only two code paths change omp or herdr config, both only on an explicit user action in the UI: `host.setupApply` (fixed fix ids only) and `omp.settingsSet`, which writes omp's global config through `omp config set\|reset` (keys checked against omp's own schema, values validated by omp, never through a shell); `hostSync.apply` (§3.24) writes the items the user chose through the same path. Credentials are write-only: never returned or logged, and never in a command line (argv is readable by every local user): batches that set one go through the companion extension's settings-write mode, values in an owner-only file under `~/.teleflock/run` that is deleted on read; without that mode (extension < 1.3.0) the write is refused and the hint points to the setting's environment override (§3.10). |
| Tool updates | Only `omp update [--check]` and plain `herdr update` (never `--handoff`, never a channel switch, stdin closed so no prompt can stop a server), as the host user, with the binaries hostd already runs, each in its own process group that is killed whole on timeout. Installs are serialized across every daemon of the user (`~/.cache/teleflock-tools.lock`). `host.toolsConfig {auto: false}` stops automatic runs per host; daemons on a home other than `~/.teleflock` (lab / dev) start with automatic runs off. |
| Copying a host's setup | `hostSync.*` (§3.24) only on an explicit apply of chosen items. What crosses the app is no secret: credential settings travel without value, MCP secrets masked, sign-ins by identity hash. Secrets the user ticks (each one separately, behind a confirm step) are sealed on the source to a one-time X25519 key of the target (HKDF-SHA256, AES-256-GCM, every routing field authenticated) and opened only there, once; a changed or replayed box applies nothing. The app routes the target's public key, so it is trusted not to swap it, as it is trusted to drive both hosts. Secrets reach omp only through stdin (`omp auth import -`), the companion extension's owner-only file (credential settings) and omp's MCP file under its lock; never argv, logs or answers. |
| Host setup installs | Only on an explicit `host.setupApply` with fixed fix ids, after the app showed what runs and where it installs and the user ticked consent (§3.12): the official installers of omp, bun and herdr over HTTPS (downloaded into the owner-only hostd home, then run as the host user), the gh release archive only when its sha256 matches the release's checksums, system packages only through the package manager when hostd runs as root or has passwordless `sudo -n`. No TTY, stdin closed, own process group, timeout, under the user-wide install lock. The only automatic file change is replacing a global bridge copy the user installed with the extension the app uploaded (never created). |
| Forwarding (§3.30) | A forward is the one place a host's processes act on another host, so it is opt-in per rule, saved on the relaying device (`forwards.json`, never on a host), off until saved, visible while in use (the Forwarding page's In use list, Stop per stream) and warned about in the editor and on the page. **What a forward grants:** everything on the from-host, its agents and what they download included, runs commands as the to-host user (shell and tools: a tool that runs other commands through its options is as good as a shell), and a forwarded port is open to every account on the from-host while the forward is on, like `ssh -L` (on Linux hostd accepts only connections of the host user's uid, on macOS and Windows hosts any local account can connect). **What the app enforces:** hosts never talk to each other, so every stream is relayed by the app; it finds the rule from the link the open arrived on (a host can only use rules whose from-host it is, never name another rule), checks the kind against what the rule allows (a command or shell needs `shell`, a tool must be in `tools`, a port connection must be one of the rule's pairs; a tool's cwd is honoured only when the rule allows the shell), takes ports from the rule (never from the open), connects `127.0.0.1`/`::1` only on the to-host, caps open streams per link and rule, and bounds every stream's unacknowledged data (256 KiB, end-to-end acks). **Endpoint:** hostd's local endpoint for `tf-forward` is user-only (unix socket 0600 in the 0700 `run/` dir; on Windows a named pipe with the current-user ACL, like the other pipes). The tool shim directory leads PATH of every TeleFlock-started agent, so a forwarded tool named like a program the agent uses itself (`git`, `sh`, `node`…) would run on the to-host; the editor warns about it. |
| TeleFlock Browser (§3.33, §5.2) | DevTools access to the user's logged-in browser is control of their accounts (mail, bank, admin consoles: whatever they are signed in to), so an agent gets it only by the user's act: an attach from the app, or an Allow on the agent's `use_user_browser` request; nothing answers a request automatically (decide for me skips it) and a deny works from any device. It is visible while it lasts (a banner per agent "<agent> on <host> can control this browser", the agent's tabs live in the panel, its card) and revocable at once (Detach, Take over a tab; "Allow once" ends with the turn; the app disconnecting, the agent's omp exiting and a detach from any device end it on the host). **Loopback only:** the browser's DevTools port listens on the device's 127.0.0.1 with `--remote-allow-origins` limited to the app's own origins (tf-devserver: its served and `--allow-origin` origins), so web pages cannot drive it; the agent's port listens on the host's 127.0.0.1 (Linux: only the host user's uid may connect; macOS and Windows: any local account, as with forwarded ports) and exists only while the attachment does. **Scoped, never the raw port:** an agent reaches only the app core's DevTools proxy, which shows it only the tabs it opened and the tabs the user lent it (other agents' and the user's own tabs do not exist for it: Chrome's own "No target with given id found"), lets Fetch, Security and Network commands act on its own tabs only, refuses browser-wide commands that would hurt the user or other agents (`Browser.close`, crashes, cookie and cache wipes, download-behaviour changes, the default context's disposal) and commands that reach the device's files (`DOM.setFileInputFiles`, `Network.loadNetworkResource`) with a CDP error (`-32000`, "TeleFlock refused `<method>` …"), never opens or navigates an agent's tab to a non-web URL (`chrome:`, `file:`, `devtools:`, extension pages), never pauses a foreign new tab, and, like Chrome, answers only requests whose `Host` is an IP address or `localhost` (DNS rebinding) and refuses WebSocket upgrades that carry an `Origin` (web pages). **Shared logins are shared risk:** the tool's result tells the model that the logins are the user's and not to sign out, switch accounts, change account settings or make payments or other irreversible changes without asking; nothing technical stops what a signed-in user could do in an allowed tab. omp's `browser.cdpUrl` is a runtime override in the agent's process only (never written to a config file), and omp's relay is off while it lasts. |
| On-device dictation (§6.16) | Dictation opens the microphone only while a session runs (recordings, §6.15, while they record, until 60 minutes at most: the app core stops a recording at its limits whether or not the page sends anything): a tapped mic, one session at a time, ended by stop, a minute without speech, the page going away (a reload, a closed devserver tab), the chosen model's deletion and shutdown; a stop, cancel or page close that crosses a start reaches the session once it started, and a cancel or page close closes the microphone at once, also while the models still load. Audio stays in the app core's memory and becomes text on the device; nothing is sent anywhere (Rambler sends the recognised text to a host only when the user turned it on, §6.10). Models come only from the catalog's pinned Hugging Face commits over HTTPS (rustls with the Mozilla roots), and a model counts as installed only after every file matched its pinned size and sha256; the engine's libraries are pinned by sha256 at build time. The fake microphone and the download mirror (`TF_DICTATION_FAKE_MIC`, `TF_DICTATION_MIRROR`) exist in debug builds only. macOS asks the user for the microphone (usage text and the `audio-input` entitlement); a refusal on macOS or Windows says where to allow it. |
